NOT A STACKING-CONTEXT TRAP (verified this session): NONE of the scrim's ancestors (`.subheader` → `.main` → `.body` → `.root` → `<body>`) carries transform / filter / will-change / opacity<1 / backdrop-filter / contain / isolation / perspective (redesign.module.css contains none of them anywhere); the fixed z-index:60 scrim is a ROOT-level layer over static/auto-z page content, so the bleed is NOT a z-index/stacking problem. FIVE PRIOR ATTEMPTS (none fixed the keyboard bleed): (1) LORAMER_MOBILE_BOTTOM_SHEET_V1 (4707757) — legacy /dashboard mobile bottom sheet "preserves visible context"; SHIPPED (legacy surface, not -next). (2) popover bottom-sheet experiment (9af6c92 "never cropped") → REVERTED (1c7273c "breaks desktop"). (3) LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_FLEX_MINWIDTH_V1 (25b4957) — min-width:0 down the flex chain + `.md` table/pre internal scrollers; SHIPPED; fixed the HORIZONTAL wide-answer overflow / send-arrow-off-screen, NOT the keyboard bleed. (4) LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_VISUAL_VIEWPORT_V1 (78de5ab) — bound `.scrim` `--chat-h`/`--chat-top` to `window.visualViewport.height`/`.offsetTop` (100dvh fallback) and `.panel`→`height:100%` → REVERTED (e1bcf75, 2026-07-17), stated reason: "did NOT fix ★UI-OVERFLOW (unchanged on device, Chrome + Safari); an unproven change carried into launch week is pure risk." (5) LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_DEBUG_V1 (130bae0 + 6cace15) — `?debug=chat` in-flow, peak-tracked horizontal-axis readout; SHIPPED; an INSTRUMENT to measure `visualViewport.offsetLeft`, NOT a fix. THE MECHANISM IS NOT YET IDENTIFIED. "dvh does not shrink for the keyboard" is a TRUE banked premise, but the one attempt that acted on it (the visualViewport binding, #4) did NOT fix the symptom on-device — so that premise is NECESSARY-NOT-SUFFICIENT and something else is in play; candidate axes NOT yet proven: iOS position:fixed behavior under the keyboard, the sheet's own scroll/overflow, the `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` input-bar padding, or a mis-identification of what "bleed" actually shows. RULE: NO SIXTH ATTEMPT until the mechanism is identified AND reproduced — five undirected attempts across two sessions is the signature of guessing (cross-ref the RULE-HOME LAW: repeat failure needs an identified cause, not another try). NEXT STEP IS A MEASUREMENT (with the keyboard OPEN: which element is visible, at what coordinates, against visualViewport vs layout viewport) BEFORE any code. This entry RECORDS the diagnosis; it fixes nothing. Cross-ref QUEUE ★UI-OVERFLOW / ★CHAT-GATE-ZINDEX (distinct symptoms). ⚠⚠ **MECHANISM MEASURED AND CONFIRMED 2026-07-26 — THIS ENTRY'S "MECHANISM NOT YET IDENTIFIED" IS SUPERSEDED. The gate it imposed (no sixth attempt without a measurement) is SATISFIED; the measurement is below and the fix is sequenced in two steps.** INSTRUMENT: LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_VIEWPORT_PROBE_V1, server-logged, iPhone iOS 26.2, **CriOS/150 (Chrome on iOS, NOT Safari — the device record said Safari and the UA says otherwise)**, route /dashboard-next/client-profile, 40+ samples. **IT IS BOTH HALVES, AND THEY ARE SEQUENTIAL:** **(a) THE KEYBOARD OCCLUDES, IT DOES NOT RESIZE.** doc.clientHeight held at **766 across every one of 40+ samples** while visualViewport.height fell **766 → 428**. So the LAYOUT viewport never changed and the keyboard occludes **338 layout px**. `.panel` at 100dvh is still 766px tall, so it overhangs the visible window by 338px — the composer and the bottom of the sheet sit UNDER the keyboard. That is the bleed. **(b) iOS AUTO-ZOOMS on the sub-16px input**, ~22ms AFTER the keyboard opens: 19:15:13.685 scale 1 / vv.height 428 / vv.width 440, then 19:15:13.707 **scale 1.1431818008422852** / vv.height 374.39 / vv.width 384.890625 / offsetTop 391.6 / offsetLeft 4.66. **PROOF IT IS A ZOOM AND NOT A RESIZE: 440 ÷ 384.890625 = 1.1431818…, matching the reported scale to SEVEN DECIMALS** — the visual viewport's WIDTH shrank by exactly the scale factor, which only a zoom does. The zoom then compounds (a), shrinking the visible window to ~374 and offsetting it ~350-390px, which is why the scrim reports y = −344 to −392 in visual coordinates. ⛔ **WHY dvh WAS NEVER GOING TO WORK — THIS AVENUE IS CLOSED PERMANENTLY.** dvh tracks the LAYOUT viewport, and the layout viewport never moved (766, constant, all samples). There was nothing for it to track. Do not propose a dvh-based fix for this symptom again. ⛔ **WHY 78de5ab FAILED, SPECIFICALLY.** It bound `--chat-h`/`--chat-top` to visualViewport.**height** and **offsetTop** only, with a 100dvh fallback. It had no knowledge of `scale`, because nothing had ever measured it — so at scale 1.143 the visual viewport is also **12.5% NARROWER (440→385) and horizontally offset (offsetLeft 4.66)**, and a height/top-only binding leaves that axis unhandled. Any step-2 fix that binds height+offsetTop WITHOUT also handling width/offsetLeft is a repeat of 78de5ab and must be refused. ⚠ **THE INSTRUMENT DEFECT THAT NEARLY BANKED A FALSE FALSIFICATION** (fixed same day, LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_PROBE_FREEZE_V1): the on-screen readout LIVE-UPDATED, so the reading relayed from the device — `scale 1.000, vvH 766 = docH = innerH` — was the **focus+600 sample from 19:15:07, SIX SECONDS BEFORE the keyboard opened**. True numbers, wrong phase. It was read as falsifying auto-zoom and an instruction was issued to bank auto-zoom as REJECTED BY MEASUREMENT; the server log said the opposite and the rejection was refused before it was written. THE LESSON, and it is the sharper form of VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT: **an instrument can be correct and still mislead if it does not tell you WHICH PHASE you are looking at.** The readout now LATCHES on the first sample where doc.clientHeight − vv.height > 100 and holds it. ⚠ **UNVERIFIED-BY-US, consistent with our own data:** published reports describe an iOS 26 bug where visualViewport.offsetTop does not reset to 0 after the keyboard is dismissed. Our log shows offsetTop stuck at 344-392 through the entire second episode. **Step 2 MUST NOT assume offsetTop returns to 0 on dismiss.** THE SEQUENCED FIX: STEP 1 = `.input` 14px→16px, removes the ZOOM trigger only (LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_INPUT_16PX_V1, shipped 2026-07-26). STEP 2 = bind the overlay to the visual viewport on height, offsetTop, AND width/offsetLeft with dvh as fallback — authored only after a device reading confirms scale holds at 1.000. **Step 1 alone does NOT close the bleed: at scale 1 with the keyboard up there is still 338px of overhang.** | LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_KEYBOARD_BLEED_V1, 2026-07-24 | do not relitigate.

(3) RBAC IS EXPLICIT + PER CALL, server-side, in the runClaudeToolLoop EXECUTOR: for every tool_use it resolves the TARGET client = the bound scope client (single-client tab — wins, and the model cannot steer it off) OR, at agency scope, the model-supplied `tu.input.clientId`; then `viewerCanAccess(userEmail, target)` runs and FAILS CLOSED (no target OR no access → an is_error tool_result, the query layer is never reached). PROSE IS FREE TO VARY; the claim is NOT. The gate tests for CONTRADICTION — opposite DIRECTION, a DIFFERENT entity named as the problem, a threshold crossed differently — NOT identical wording. PREREQUISITE (load-bearing): the JUDGMENT BASIS must be fixed in DATA first — value_model, targets, thresholds. A determinism test over UNSTABLE ground measures the GROUND, not the model — an unset value_model makes "is this ROAS good" non-reproducible BY CONSTRUCTION (see QUEUE ★VALUE-MODEL-COVERAGE-GAP: 11 of 28 active clients have no value_model). (2) GOOGLE ADS — 30 DAYS default; conversion data can change for up to 90 DAYS after the fact (late conversions, attribution recalculation). That capability is supplied by a THIRD-PARTY, MERCHANT-INSTALLED WordPress plugin ("Products and Orders Last Modified for WC REST API"), i.e. something we neither control nor can require. On wc/v3 the `after` / `before` params filter **date_created**, not date_modified. Our fetcher is therefore CORRECT as written, not lagging: `fetchWooOrdersRaw` (src/lib/intelligence/woocommerce-intelligence.ts:342-400) builds `/orders?after=…&before=…&status=any` because no reliable modified-date path exists to use. Its coverage is bounded by whatever created-date window we choose, and **a refund on an order created outside that window is never caught** — a real, permanent gap that must not be described as bounded-but-fine. Woo Tier-1 as a fixed created-date window is therefore NOT a Shopify-copying shortcut; it is the ONLY implementable mechanism. Durable fix = ★ORDER-LEVEL-STORAGE (store the order grain → re-fetch by order id detects change with no modified-date filter at all). [build status → FLEET STATUS 2026-07-25 below] DESIGN PATTERN to follow: TWO TIERS — a cheap FREQUENT pass over recent days, PLUS one deeper ROLLBACK per day; NOT one expensive window on every run. Cause: Next.js 14's App Router patches global `fetch` and caches GET requests by default; supabase-js reads are GETs. The FIRST answer — legitimately empty, taken before any op existed — was replayed for every later check. FOUR bulk operations were started where ONE was allowed, **and nothing errored**: the check ran, returned a valid empty array, and was wrong. Same shape as every silent-substitution defect in this repo — a green signal answering a question about a different moment in time. FIX: a dedicated `sbNoStore` client in the adapter, used for every gating read (in-flight check, drain selection, gid lookup, connection lookup); the shared supabaseAdmin is untouched everywhere else. GUARDED: leg 3 of order-grain-writer.guard fails the build if the in-flight check stops using it. ⚠ WIDER IMPLICATION, FLAGGED AND NOT SCOPED — every other supabaseAdmin READ inside a route handler is subject to the same default. Production hides it (each invocation is a fresh process AND most reads are not gating a write), which is exactly why it would surface as a rare, unreproducible wrong answer rather than an error. That is the STRONGER instrument, not a fallback: n_tup_ins/upd/del are exact counts of rows inserted, updated, and deleted, so identical values prove zero writes, where a matching count(*) would only prove the net was zero. **store_bulk_operations STAYS — Russ's call: one-op-per-shop plus serverless means the writer must know whether an op is in flight, and nothing else can hold that state.** NOTHING READS THESE TABLES — no writer, no adapter, no reader shipped; applying the migration alone changed zero behavior, which the counter proof confirms. NEXT BUILD IS BLOCKED ON A DECISION, not on effort: ★SHOPIFY-API-VERSION-SUNSET must be settled first, because the polling shape (`bulkOperation(id:)` on 2026-01+ vs `currentBulkOperation` below it) and the concurrency limit (five vs one) both fall out of the version pin. Revert remains a clean DROP in FK order: store_order_line_items → store_bulk_operations → store_orders. ORIGINAL DESIGN RATIONALE, unchanged: THREE NEW TABLES, additive only, no ALTER/DROP/backfill, nothing reads them until the writer ships, so applying the migration alone changes zero behavior; revert = DROP (line items first, FK). `store_orders` + `store_order_line_items` are PLATFORM-AGNOSTIC on the universal key (client_id, platform, account_id) + vendor order id — Shopify and Woo share the SCHEMA and do NOT share an ADAPTER (Shopify via Bulk Operations JSONL, Woo via paged REST + re-fetch-by-id), which is the platform-extensibility law applied as written. `store_bulk_operations` is the third table and is FLAGGED for Russ to strike: the Bulk Operations API is asynchronous and we run serverless, so an op started in one invocation finishes in another, and sync_state has no home for "op gid X is RUNNING". THE LOAD-BEARING DESIGN DECISION: **created_date is adapter-written, NOT a generated column.** Shopify's createdAt carries the SHOP's UTC offset and the live path buckets on the shop-local date; a `generated always as ((created_at at time zone 'UTC')::date)` column would disagree by one day for every late-evening order and every recompute-from-local would then contradict rows forward capture already wrote — byte-identical-to-forward-capture is banked law. created_at_raw preserves the vendor string so a disagreement is provable rather than assumed away. Line items are REPLACE-PER-ORDER inside one transaction, never upserted, because an order edit can REMOVE a line and an upsert only overwrites keys that recur — the same stale-key trap already live at cron/sync:319, deliberately not reproduced at the new grain. Disappearance is detected by last_seen_at + a deleted_upstream_at TOMBSTONE, never by hard-deleting the row: absence must be evidence, not silence. VOLUME MEASURED, not estimated: 20,965 Shopify orders all-time across 7 clients (largest 18,620) + 4,440 Woo = ~25.4k orders fleet-wide, ~46k line items at 1.8/order — roughly 120 MB with raw jsonb, against Supabase Pro's 8 GB included disk, so ~$0 incremental and ~2 orders of magnitude of headroom. Small compute (2 GB RAM, 2-core ARM, $15/mo) is irrelevant to the storage and relevant only to the 8s PostgREST ceiling, which the partial day index exists to keep clear. | LORAMER_ORDER_LEVEL_STORAGE_V1, 2026-07-25 | do not relitigate.

- [LAW 2026-07-25] **LORAMER_VERIFIED_PLATFORM_SCOPE_V1 — a law asserting per-platform API behavior must NAME which platforms were actually verified and which were INFERRED. An inferred platform is UNVERIFIED until checked.** PROVENANCE: LORAMER_RESTATEMENT_WINDOW_LAW_V1 clause (4) was written 2026-07-24 from vendor research, grouped as "SHOPIFY / WOO", and asserted native `updated_at` change-based sync for BOTH. The research covered Shopify. Woo was GENERALIZED from it and never checked — and Woo has no native modified-date filter at all, so the clause committed us on paper to a Tier-2 leg that cannot be built. Nothing failed and nothing looked wrong: the law read confident, the grouping read natural, and the next session would have planned unbuildable work off it. **THIS IS THE SAME FAILURE SHAPE AS THE 2026-07-25 fetchErrors HOLE** — correct in one place (the cron consumed it), assumed everywhere else (the prompt renderer never did) — and as the 2026-07-15 registry claim that Google age/gender was "VERIFIED in-code" when only the FETCH was verified and persistence had never landed. THE RULE: when a law states what an API does, each platform named carries its own tag — **[VERIFIED <date>, <what was checked>]** or **[INFERRED — UNVERIFIED]**. A shared clause covering N platforms needs N tags, not one. Grouping platforms by similarity is how one platform's behavior becomes another platform's fiction. COROLLARY: "both stores work the same way" is a hypothesis, never a finding — the whole point of MULTI-SOURCE METRIC PROVENANCE is that sources differ, and that applies to their APIs as much as to their numbers. | LORAMER_VERIFIED_PLATFORM_SCOPE_V1, 2026-07-25 | do not relitigate.

SHAPE: two-tier per the law's own design note — a cheap FREQUENT pass over ~7 trailing created-date days on the normal forward run, PLUS one deeper ~90-day rollback once daily, off-peak. Rationale: with no modified-date filter the ONLY lever on coverage is window width, and a single wide window on every run would hammer a merchant's WordPress host for almost no marginal catch. MANDATORY, not optional: the full LIVE-SOURCE PRINCIPLE stack — circuit breaker, throttle (spaced pages; Woo forward currently passes `throttleMs = 0`, which is right for a one-day fetch and WRONG for a multi-week window), adaptive sub-chunk ladder, graceful-200. **THE SHELLEY LOAD INCIDENT IS WHY THE THROTTLE IS NOT NEGOTIABLE** — a self-hosted merchant's server is not a managed API with a quota to bounce off; it is someone's live storefront, and the failure mode is their customers' checkout getting slow, which we would never see in our own logs. PATTERN PARITY: one windowed fetch bucketed by created date (never N per-day fetches), rows built per day, upsert-REPLACE on the 7-col conflict key, per-day try/catch, the dry-run reading stored per-day account revenue to SHOW drift rather than assert it — all lifted from Shopify Tier-1 (69e0d52). REJECTED — it is not a dependency, and adopting it means handing `streamText.maxSteps` the tool loop, which is where the per-call `viewerCanAccess` RBAC lives; it would also re-express the cache_control prefix block through a different provider abstraction and rewire the model chain shipped hours earlier (bf184a4). That is a rewrite of the security-critical half to buy ~30 lines of framing. Hand-rolled the framing, kept the loop. **TWO REAL FAILURES CAUGHT IN GATE-A, both the same disease — shipping the SHAPE of streaming with none of its value:** (1) the first cut buffered each turn and flushed once → measured first-byte 66.8s of a 67.0s turn; fixed by emitting deltas from the SDK's `text` handler as they arrive. (2) the second cut still awaited the whole chain BEFORE constructing the ReadableStream, so every frame queued and replayed → measured **152 deltas, 13,124 chars, all inside a 332ms window at the tail of a 125s turn — a 0% progressive window**. Fixed by constructing the stream FIRST, capturing its controller synchronously, running the loop into the LIVE controller, and committing the Response on a first-token-or-settle race. MEASURED AFTER: first text 16.7s, last delta 103.7s, total 104.0s ⇒ **87.3s progressive window = 84% of the turn.** CHANNEL DISTINCTION: intermediate-turn text is preamble (the blocking loop discards it), so a `tool` event tells the client to demote the live buffer to a status line; the authoritative `answer` event carries the final text, so the PERSISTED answer stays byte-identical to the blocking path's finalResp. THE SSE/ERROR-JSON FOOTGUN, handled explicitly: the Response is not returned until first token or chain-settle, so auth 401 / RBAC 404 / missing-message 400 / overloaded 503 all still return ordinary JSON with real status codes; only a POST-token failure degrades to an SSE `error` event, which is honest because the user has already seen text. logSpend moved INSIDE the stream close path and is AWAITED before the controller closes — the blocking route's fire-and-forget-after-return is exactly why it died on the serverless freeze (ECONNRESET 22:31:46, UND_ERR_SOCKET 16:43:27); PROVEN, four spend rows landed from streamed Gate-A turns including a 25,315-in/9,435-out one. IDLE-GAP ABORT replaces total-duration: 45s of silence, re-armed on every SSE event, so a legitimately long multi-tool answer never trips it while a dropped connection is caught; with the flag off nothing re-arms and it degrades to the original 120s total cap. ALL THREE consumers migrated in this commit through ONE dual-mode reader (src/lib/chat-stream-read.ts) that branches on the RESPONSE's content-type, not a build-time constant — so the flag flips server-side with no client redeploy, and the two LEGACY dashboard call sites (page.tsx:959, :3776) are done here rather than left as the easy-to-forget half recon flagged. EVALS NOT RUN, verified not asserted: build-claude-context.ts and source-parity.ts UNCHANGED, and the streaming loop builds the SAME createParams (model/max_tokens/system/messages/tools) as the blocking loop — transport changed, prompt did not. | LORAMER_CHAT_STREAMING_V1, 2026-07-25 | do not relitigate.

Classifier reads the SDK's TYPED surface (status 529 / error.type 'overloaded_error'), never err.message string-matching. **ONLY overload advances the chain** — a 4xx/auth/tool failure surfaces as itself, because another model fails identically and the budget would be spent learning it. Same law as LORAMER_LIVE_VS_CAPTURED_ARE_TWO_SOURCES_V1 — never silently substitute a source. logSpend records the **ANSWERING** model, never the primary constant (a fallback turn billed at the primary's rate is a FALSE COST, silent and permanent). Every 529 logs model + Anthropic request_id so recurrence rate is MEASURABLE — we still do not know if 529s are rare or chronic. All three overloaded ⇒ 503 + error:'overloaded', never silent degradation. FIVE DISTINCT client strings, no fallthrough (client-not-found · overloaded-after-chain · server-error · our-timeout · connection-dropped) — before this, a busy model and a real bug rendered the SAME sentence, so neither the user nor a screenshot could tell them apart. Never hide, blend, collapse, or silently pick one … Lora holds all sources at once, distinguishes them, and explains WHY they differ") applied to the live-vs-captured axis, exactly as it already applies to Shopify-vs-GA revenue and to the three ROAS bases. RUSS, VERBATIM: "It depends on the question, AND we have to start moving faster and better because the LIVE environment is in the queue. So it's not either or, it has to be determined by what question is being asked AND in most cases for honesty it should be BOTH." THREE THINGS THIS SETTLES: (1) NOT EITHER/OR — live-vs-captured is not a pick; where both exist and can differ, BOTH are surfaced, each labeled with its source and its as_of/settlement basis. (2) QUESTION-DETERMINED — which source LEADS is a function of the question asked (a "right now / today" question leads live; a settled-history question leads captured), so the routing is not a fixed global default. (3) BOTH IS THE HONESTY DEFAULT — in most cases both are shown; showing one silently is the failure mode, and it is the same class as the false zero closed by LORAMER_LORA_FETCHERRORS_DEGRADED_V1. ⚠ THIS REFINES A LOCKED DESIGN, stated plainly rather than slipped in: docs/LORAMER_LIVE_BREADTH_UNIFIED_DESIGN.md §4 (LOCKED 2026-06-26) reads "CAPTURED-BY-DEFAULT: Lora answers from the captured system-of-record unless the question is time-sensitive" + offer-live-on-relevance. Russ's statement STRENGTHENS the default from captured-first-then-offer toward both-where-both-exist. Russ owns DESTINATION, so this is his call to make and it stands; §4's captured-by-default is hereby the FALLBACK for families that exist in only one source, not the general answer shape. Everything else in that design is UNCHANGED and still governs — Direction B, the provability wall (§2, live writers must never touch the metrics_daily upsert), and above all §3 MERGE WITH MANDATORY PROVENANCE: a BARE combined figure fusing live and captured with no disclosure remains FORBIDDEN, and "BOTH" means both LABELED, never both AVERAGED. WHY THEY DIFFER is the payload, and it is already researched — LORAMER_RESTATEMENT_WINDOW_LAW_V1 owns the settlement windows (Google 30d default / conversions up to 90d · Meta 9d min, 28d with 28-day attribution · GA 7d processing · Shopify/Woo change-based on updated_at). Build shape + blast radius: QUEUE ★LIVE-VS-CAPTURED-DUAL-RENDER. | LORAMER_LIVE_VS_CAPTURED_ARE_TWO_SOURCES_V1, 2026-07-25 | do not relitigate.

The USER turn write (ChatLauncher.tsx:182) is UNTOUCHED this slice. - [SHIPPED 2026-07-26 — LAW-ENFORCING] **LORAMER_CHAT_ANSWER_RECOVERY_V1 — the client stops claiming an answer was lost, classifies aborts on the signal it OWNS, and RECOVERS the persisted answer instead of asking for a re-ask.** AMENDS (does not duplicate) LORAMER_CHAT_CLIENT_ABORT_V1 and LORAMER_CHAT_FAILURE_BRANCHES_V1; builds on LORAMER_CHAT_SERVER_TURN_WRITE_V1. THE DEFECT: after slice 1 the answer was durable server-side, but ChatLauncher still rendered "I couldn't reach Lora just now — the connection dropped before I got an answer back" on turns the server COMPLETED and PERSISTED. Observed live twice on 2026-07-26; the copy caused three re-asks at ~$0.50 each. **WEB-FIRST FINDING [VERIFIED 2026-07-26, published sources] — the obvious fix was WRONG.** On iOS Safari an aborted fetch does NOT reliably reject with a DOMException named 'AbortError': WebKit surfaces a generic `TypeError: Load failed`, and it surfaces THE SAME ERROR for genuine network failures, so the two are INDISTINGUISHABLE by the error object. Also string-matching "Load failed" would therefore misclassify real network drops as aborts. Sources: developer.apple.com/forums/thread/771127 · github.com/hotwired/stimulus/issues/782 · trackjs.com/javascript-errors/load-failed/ · developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/AbortSignal. THE CORRECT DISCRIMINATOR is the AbortSignal we own — `controller.signal.aborted`, set synchronously by our own abort() call, spec'd, supported across browsers since 2018. NEVER string-match the message to decide this. THE RECOVERY DISCRIMINATOR, and its stated limit: there is no turn id (that needs a migration), so recovery uses a MONOTONIC WATERMARK — the max conversation row id known before the turn was sent, captured from the hydration read that already runs on open. An assistant row with a HIGHER id was written after we asked, so an older turn can never be re-rendered. ⚠ A WATERMARK IS NOT A TURN ID: if a second tab asked the same client in the same window, its answer would also clear the watermark. That case is DETECTABLE (more than one new assistant row) and is REFUSED with an explicit "I will not guess which one is yours", never guessed. No watermark or no clientId ⇒ no recovery attempted. Recovery is a READ (GET /api/conversations); it NEVER re-POSTs /api/chat, because a silent retry doubles the spend on a turn that most likely already succeeded. GET CONTRACT VERIFIED LIVE before shipping: accepts clientId+surface, returns a NUMERIC monotonic `id`, and is RBAC-scoped by `user_email = session.user.email` (a different caller got 0 rows; no cookie got 401). ⚠ STATED CEILING, not fixed here: that same GET is `.order(created_at asc).limit(200)`, the SIBLING of ★LORA-MEMORY-OLDEST-500 — past 200 rows per (client, user, surface) it returns the OLDEST 200 and recovery would silently find nothing. Measured 2026-07-26: the largest (client, surface) pair is 87 rows, so it does not bite today. TIMERS, revised against MEASURED durations rather than estimates (2026-07-26 production, Veterinary mastermind, user row → awaited spend row): 125.6s · 105.3s · 78.2s, plus a 26.9s Gate-B turn. CHAT_TOTAL_MS 120s→240s (1.91x the worst measured; deliberately UNDER the route's maxDuration of 300s so the SERVER stays the limiter and the client never waits on a killed lambda). CHAT_IDLE_GAP_MS 45s→150s — ⚠ HONEST BASIS: there is NO per-event gap instrumentation, so the only defensible bound is that a silent stretch cannot exceed the whole turn, hence idle ≥ the longest turn measured; 150 is DERIVED from the total-turn bound, NOT measured directly. DUAL-DEADLINE FIX (correct for when streaming returns): the old rearmIdle REPLACED the total cap, so once the first event arrived the absolute ceiling vanished and a stream dripping one byte every 40s ran forever; the deadline is now set once and never re-armed, every re-arm is clamped to it, and the idle timer arms ONLY after the first event so a healthy 200s blocking turn is never killed by an idle gap it was not in. GATE-A 59/59 against the real files; TWO failures found and fixed rather than argued away — idle 120s failed against the 125.6s measurement (raised to 150s), and my first dual-deadline model armed the idle timer from t=0, which would have introduced a regression the old code did not have. GATE-B 17/17 on the real route + real prod DB: a client aborted at 1,211ms with a real AbortError, and recovery FOUND and RENDERED the persisted answer (rows 542 user → 543 assistant), exactly one bubble, checking-copy replaced not appended; an abort with nothing persisted produced honest copy and no phantom; 3 POSTs to /api/chat and 3 spend rows, so recovery cost nothing beyond the original turns. | LORAMER_CHAT_ANSWER_RECOVERY_V1, 2026-07-26 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-26 — TRACK 1 OF 2] **LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_FULLSCREEN_V1 — the -next Ask-Lora sheet stops taking the page with it: one scroll container, contained overscroll, a locked body, a portaled overlay, and a back gesture that closes the chat instead of leaving the route.** SCOPE, STATED SO IT IS NOT MISREAD: this is the SCROLL/CONTAINMENT half. It does NOT fix the iOS keyboard bleed — that mechanism is still unidentified and remains blocked behind LORAMER_NEXT_CHAT_KEYBOARD_BLEED_V1's rule (no sixth attempt without a measurement). THE MECHANISMS, identified this session and each fixed at its cause: (1) the chat chain had TWO live vertical scrollers — `.scroll` and the document — and `overscroll-behavior` appeared NOWHERE in the -next chain (repo-wide grep found one hit, in legacy dashboard/page.tsx:109), so a drag past either edge of the message list CHAINED to the page underneath; it also explains the track/thumb mismatch, because the visible scrollbar belonged to the document, whose scroll range is far larger than the list's. FIX: `overscroll-behavior: contain` on `.scroll` PLUS a body scroll lock that restores the PRIOR inline value on close (restoring a hardcoded '' would clobber whatever else owned body.overflow). (2) the chat `.scrim` sat at z-index 60, TIED with `.menu` (redesign.module.css:404), winning only because ChatLauncher renders after TopBar in Shell — an accident of source order, not a design. FIX: 60 → 100, its own band above every fixed layer (.menuBackdrop 55, .drawer 50, nav scrim 40, MobileNav 30). (3) there was NO `viewport` export anywhere in src/app, so Next emitted only its default. FIX: width/initialScale/interactiveWidget:'resizes-content' — ⚠ Chromium/Android ONLY, Safari ignores it; it is NOT an iOS fix. (4) `.head` had no top safe-area inset while the sheet is full-screen, so the header sat under the notch (the composer already cleared the home indicator). (5) the overlay was not portaled — no ancestor defeats position:fixed today (re-verified down the whole chain at HEAD), but a portal makes that permanent instead of a fact that must be re-verified every time Shell changes; `createPortal` preserves React TREE position, so ChatLauncher does NOT remount and the d55f739 cross-client bleed cannot return (asserted in Gate-A). (6) BACK now closes the chat: one tagged history entry pushed on open, popstate closes, and an X/scrim/Esc close CONSUMES our own entry so no phantom survives to swallow a later back press. ⛔ **maximumScale: 1 WAS PROPOSED AND IS REJECTED — DO NOT RE-PROPOSE IT.** It would stop iOS auto-zoom on input focus, but it blocks PINCH-ZOOM for every user, which is a real accessibility regression on a data product built around dense tables. The same result comes from a 16px font-size on the chat input, which is Track 2 and waits on its measurement. Russ ruled on this 2026-07-26. JUDGMENT CALL, OWNED AND STATED: the spec said "exactly ONE scroll container", and the composer `<textarea>` is technically a second one — it grows to max-height then scrolls its own draft. That behaviour is CORRECT (it is P-PL#3's growing input; a textarea that cannot scroll hides text the user typed), so it was KEPT and given the same `overscroll-behavior: contain` rather than removed. The guard counts LAYOUT scrollers and asserts the textarea is contained. - [LAW / CLASS 2026-07-26] **LORAMER_PORTAL_SEVERS_CSS_VARS_V1 — A REACT PORTAL TO document.body SEVERS CSS CUSTOM PROPERTY INHERITANCE. CSS custom properties inherit down the DOM TREE; `createPortal` relocates the subtree out of that tree, so `var(--x)` resolves to nothing and the declaration becomes invalid at computed-value time — `background: var(--accent)` silently becomes TRANSPARENT rather than erroring. WHAT IT COST: the -next chat send button rendered as a WHITE glyph in a TRANSPARENT circle on a WHITE bar — present, enabled, 38x38, correctly positioned, fully clickable, and INVISIBLE. Nobody could send a message from mobile Lora. Measured in WebKit: `--accent` = #2563eb on `.root`, `(UNRESOLVED)` on the overlay; `.sendBtn` background rgba(0,0,0,0), color rgb(255,255,255), bar rgb(255,255,255). Collateral in the same subtree: `.head`/`.inputBar` borders rendered near-black instead of pale, `.bubbleAssistant` background transparent, `.close`/`.emptyLead`/`.headTitle i` all dead. ⚠ **EXPOSURE, RECORDED HONESTLY: invisible in PRODUCTION from f71d501 (~17:59Z) through four deployments — roughly five and a half hours.** It survived a revert because the revert targeted step 2, which never caused it. Bisect: c91b720 portal=0 VISIBLE → f71d501 portal=2 INVISIBLE → adc7ced / d65d7a7 / 6447b6b all still broken. ⚠ **THE PORTAL WAS CLAUDE'S CALL AND ITS COST WAS NEVER ASSESSED.** It was proposed in the Track 1 recon as 'cheap permanent immunity to any future ancestor gaining a transform', Russ upgraded it from optional to required on that framing, and neither of us asked what moving the subtree out of `.root` would take with it. The recon named one benefit and zero costs. A structural relocation is not free and must be costed before it is authorised. ⚠ **FOUR GEOMETRY-ONLY HARNESSES COULD NOT HAVE CAUGHT IT.** Track 1's Gate-A, step 2's Gate-A, the step-1 Gate-A and the diagnostic all measured rects — width, height, position, in-bounds — and all were correctly green, because the GEOMETRY IS PERFECT. Not one read a colour. **THE FOURTH GATE-A CLAUSE, now standing for every chat surface: the send affordance must EXIST, have NON-ZERO SIZE, sit FULLY IN-BOUNDS, and be VISUALLY DISTINGUISHABLE FROM ITS BACKGROUND — computed contrast >= 3:1 — asserted in WEBKIT, not Chromium.** Chromium was green on this bug all day. THE FIX (shipped this commit): the token declarations are extracted from `.root` into a `.tokens` class carrying NOTHING else, `.root` COMPOSES it (one source of truth, no duplicated values), and the portaled scrim carries `.tokens` alongside `.scrim`. ⛔ THE OBVIOUS FIX — putting `.root` itself on the portal container — WAS REJECTED AFTER CHECKING IT, at Russ's explicit instruction to verify before assuming: `.root` also carries display:flex, flex-direction:column, min-height:100vh, background:var(--paper), color, font-family, line-height and font-smoothing, so it would have injected a 100vh paper-coloured flex column into <body>. Token-only scope is the correct shape. A page rendered as Shell's `{children}` sits inside that clip, so the document cannot scroll and the probe's validated pattern is dead on arrival. THE RESOLUTION, verified against the code rather than assumed: **`Shell` is a COMPONENT each page imports, NOT the layout.** The auth and onboarding gate is `src/app/dashboard-next/layout.tsx` — `isPreviewUser()` then `enforceWelcomeGate()` — which applies to EVERY route under /dashboard-next regardless of whether the page renders Shell. - [LESSON-CLASS 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_GLOBAL_CSS_OVERRIDES_LOCAL_INTENT_V1 — a global stylesheet rule can silently invert a local API call, and the code will still read as if it did what it says.** `globals.css:126` sets `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }`; per spec `scrollTo({behavior:'auto'})` means "use the computed scroll-behavior" — so every scroll on the Lora page that its own comments called INSTANT was ANIMATED. MEASURED on a 26,677px thread: one call crawled 25911 → 25870 → 25772 → 25602 and was still moving 3.7 SECONDS later, under the user's finger, and it was a live contributor to "the scroll fights me". The code had said `'auto'` since the page shipped and nobody read it as a question. RULE: on any surface that manages its own scroll, `'auto'` is banned and `'instant'` is the only value that ignores the CSS — GUARDED in chat-scroll-chain.guard, conditioned on globals actually setting smooth, so it cannot become a rule about nothing. | LORAMER_GLOBAL_CSS_OVERRIDES_LOCAL_INTENT_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.
- [LESSON-CLASS 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_LAGGING_EVENT_CANNOT_GATE_A_SYNCHRONOUS_CONSUMER_V1 — state derived from an event is stale for everything that runs before the event lands, and the window is big enough to lose in.** The stick-to-bottom pin was updated from the `scroll` event. INSTRUMENTED IN WEBKIT (window.scrollTo wrapped, stacks captured): after a scroll to the top of a 23,650px thread the ResizeObserver callback fired at t+111ms and the scroll event did not arrive until t+134ms — so the follow read a stale `true` in the gap and dragged the user straight back down. The yank arrived THROUGH the machinery built to prevent it. TWO COROLLARIES, both banked: a gate must consult the SYNCHRONOUS truth (here: is the view now above where WE last put it — decidable in the same tick, and immune to "content grew below me", which a distance test is not); and **a pin that governs only the DECISION to scroll, and not the scrolls already scheduled, is not a pin** — `bottom()`'s deferred rAF/+250ms retries had to obey it too. | LORAMER_LAGGING_EVENT_CANNOT_GATE_A_SYNCHRONOUS_CONSUMER_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_SHOPIFY_DEPTH_NOTNULL_FIX_V1 — the Shopify depth write had been 23502-ing every night since 2026-07-19, and the account row's freshness hid it.** ROOT CAUSE, and it is a platform behaviour worth never relearning: **PostgREST builds ONE column list from the UNION of keys across a bulk payload, and any object missing a key that a SIBLING object supplies is sent as an explicit NULL — NOT the column DEFAULT.** `buildShopifyDepthRows` omitted `conversions` on product_type / product_vendor (intelligence-types.ts:418-419 — those two capture shapes carry `{value, netRevenue}` and no unit count, unlike product_tag which has `units`) while every sibling family set it, so Postgres rejected the WHOLE statement: `null value in column "conversions" of relation "metrics_daily" violates not-null constraint`. MEASURED IN PROD off Vercel's 7-day error-cluster table: 32 rejections naming `conversions`, plus 16 naming `spend` on days whose payload also carried a discount/abandoned row that DID set spend — four stores (Foam OH, Influential Drones, The Escential Group, Cozy Foam Factory), thirteen order-days with real money and no depth row beneath them. IT WAS INVISIBLE BY CONSTRUCTION: cron/sync writes the account row and the depth rows as TWO SEPARATE upserts (:319 and :335), the depth one inside its own try/catch that logs and continues — so the account row landed, connection health stayed green, sync_state advanced, cron returned 200, and `cron_runs.error_count` read 23 against 6 errored connections with nobody reading the difference. THE FIX, two files: (1) `conversions: 0` on the two rows — explicit 0, because there is genuinely no count in scope and the KEY'S PRESENCE is the load-bearing part; (2) `normalizeMetricsRows` union-fills any NOT-NULL numeric that a sibling row supplies. **UNION-SCOPED ON PURPOSE — never "always set all six":** filling a column absent from the ENTIRE payload would ADD it to the column list, and on a merge-duplicates upsert that turns a column the writer deliberately left untouched into one overwritten with 0 on every conflict. THE GUARD (same commit, FIX-WITH-GUARD): `tests/guards/metrics-payload-uniformity.guard.mjs`, wired into `npm run guard`. It DERIVES payload shapes BY EXECUTING the five real builders (a text scan cannot see which rows land in the SAME statement), reads the NOT-NULL set from information_schema via a committed snapshot, exempts nullable columns BY NULLABILITY not by name, and covers forward + backfill shapes on all five platforms. PROVEN RED THEN GREEN: pre-fix builder + pre-fix normalize → exit 1, 16 violations; post-fix builder + pre-fix normalize → exit 1, 12 (the guard independently confirming file 1 alone was insufficient); both post-fix → exit 0. EQUIVALENCE PROVEN BEFORE WIDENING THE BLAST RADIUS: the two normalizers are byte-identical on every payload that writes today — 5/8 executed platform payloads identical, the 3 that differ are exactly the 3 that currently throw, and a 60,000-payload property run returned 0 writing-payloads changed. **WOOCOMMERCE CARRIED THE IDENTICAL DEFECT** (spend/impressions/clicks/conversion_value on 3 of 13 rows, and its single-statement upsert would have lost the ACCOUNT row too) — closed by the same normalize change with zero edits to the Woo builder; it had simply never fired because no Woo store has had an order since the writer shipped. | LORAMER_SHOPIFY_DEPTH_NOTNULL_FIX_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.
- [LAW 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_LANDING_IS_THE_ONLY_SHIPPED_V1 — a capture family is not SHIPPED until rows are COUNTED IN metrics_daily. Fetch-measured is not landing-measured; builder-measured is not landing-measured.** PROVENANCE, and it is the whole 2026-07-19 batch: twelve Shopify and ten WooCommerce families were recorded ✅ SHIPPED on evidence that was real, specific, quantitative — and about the wrong thing. Every number banked that day measured the VENDOR RESPONSE or the in-memory aggregation: "measured 7.3× over net", "1,036 points and Shopify REJECTS it", "measured 4.43× over net, up to 11 categories on one product", "0 of 71 tagged", "UNKNOWN came out 0.00%", "reconcile EXACT ($8,609/66 orders)". Not one of them was `select count(*) from metrics_daily where breakdown_type = …`. All twenty-two held ZERO ROWS for eight days and every gate read green, because `check-capture-completeness.mjs` compares a manifest to a registry and never opens the database — which its own header says. THE RULE: the ship report for any capture family states the ROW COUNT, the DATE RANGE and the CLIENT COUNT from metrics_daily, or the family is UNPROVEN and is recorded as such. A probe proves the vendor serves it. A Gate-A proves the builder emits it. Only a row count proves we HAVE it. THE ENFORCER already exists as a queued build: ★DECLARED-VS-LANDED-CHECK (fail when a registry-declared family with a writer has zero rows fleet-wide) — this law is the reason it stops being optional, and per RULE-HOME LAW this entry is the LAST time it may be banked as prose. | LORAMER_LANDING_IS_THE_ONLY_SHIPPED_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

- [PRODUCT POSITION — RUSS, 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_LATENCY_IS_DILIGENCE_V1 — ~10 seconds to an answer is ACCEPTABLE and is positioned as a FEATURE: thorough beats instant. Wrong answers are not acceptable. UNEXPLAINED SILENCE is the actual bug.** THE CONSEQUENCE IS A STANDING PROHIBITION, and it binds route decisions: do NOT cut tool calls, shrink windows, or downgrade the model to shave seconds — that trades away the differentiator to fix a symptom that is not the problem. Lora reads more data than the instant answer can, and that IS the product. What must change is not the duration but the EXPERIENCE of it: a wait with no signal reads as a hang, and a hang reads as broken. The status indicator (★CHAT-STATUS-INDICATOR) is what converts the wait into visible diligence, which is why it sits on the 2026-09-30 demo critical path and why streaming — its prerequisite — stopped being a flag decision. Cross-ref LORAMER_DETERMINISM_OF_JUDGMENT (ESSENCE): correctness and repeatability are the promises; speed never was. | LORAMER_LATENCY_IS_DILIGENCE_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

Identical shape to LORAMER_SHOPIFY_VERSION_PIN_2026_07_V1 (sent 2025-01, served 2025-10, on all six shops, for months). ⛔ **SEAM CHECK — NO DATA DAMAGE FOUND.** Looked for a definitional step at 2026-06-09 (when everything below v24.0 was deprecated) and at the two pin-edit dates 2026-05-20 and 2026-07-24. **The prime suspect is structurally cleared: Meta is retiring the 7-day-VIEW and 28-day-VIEW windows and WE HAVE NEVER CAPTURED EITHER** — every attribution_window row across all four grains and the family's whole life (2024-11-25→2026-07-26) is one of exactly 28d_click (87,321) · 7d_click (86,980) · 1d_click (86,192) · 1d_view (27,768). A retirement of windows we never held cannot move our numbers. All four survivors continue at flat rows/day across the boundary. The one alarming reading — Veterinary conversions 555→274 on flat spend — is a SPIKE RETURNING TO BASELINE, not a step: deep-before (04-14→05-11) conv/$ 0.1098 · near-before (05-12→06-08) 0.2229 · after (06-09→07-06) **0.1094**, with spend identical to within 0.6% across all three. A definitional seam moves a level and keeps it moved. **LIMITS OF THAT VERDICT, banked so it is never over-read:** only ONE client (Veterinary) has enough conversion volume to detect a level shift at all; the pooled fleet series is CONFOUNDED by cohort growth (5→8→11→13 Meta clients across the window) and was discarded rather than used; `reach` has no testable pre-boundary history (first captured 2026-06-01, eight days before); and Meta's 9-day base restatement re-walk makes the boundary fuzzy by ~9 days. **This can rule out a large, obvious break. It cannot rule out a shift under roughly 20%.** CONSEQUENCE, stated so the work is scoped correctly: **the Meta version work is HYGIENE, not repair.** No data damage found; fix it because an unknown serving version is not an acceptable place to stand, not because the numbers are wrong. | probe 2026-07-27, LORAMER_META_SILENT_VERSION_UPGRADE_V1 | do not relitigate.

Not one drifted from correct into incorrect through neglect.** git blame, with the vendor's current version on each write date: Meta **v21.0 written 2026-07-24 — SIX WEEKS AFTER every version below v24.0 was deprecated on 9 June 2026**, vendor current v25.0 · Meta **v18.0 written into connection-probe.ts 2026-06-24 — FIFTEEN DAYS AFTER v18 left support**, vendor current v25.0 · Google Ads **`google-ads-api ^23.0.0` pinned 2026-05-11 when v24 had already shipped 22 April 2026** (and v25 landed July, so it is now two majors behind, sunsetting ~Feb 2027) · Shopify **2026-07 written 2026-07-26, current on the day — the only clean pin, and only because that commit was fixing an earlier drift** · GA4 v1beta and Woo wc/v3 are current and cannot drift. **THE CONSEQUENCE IS THE WHOLE POINT AND IT CHANGES THE GUARD: a drift guard keyed on SUNSET DATES IS THE WRONG GUARD.** A sunset-date check assumes the pin was right when written and went stale with time; every case here falsifies that. The guard must fire at **WRITE TIME** — the moment a version string enters the codebase behind current — because that is when the defect is actually introduced. - [LAW 2026-07-27] **LORAMER_VERSION_BUMP_IS_A_CAPTURE_EVENT_V1 — an API version bump is a CAPTURE-AFFECTING CHANGE and carries the same discipline as a writer change.** THE DISCIPLINE, four parts, none optional: (1) DIFF THE FIELD SEMANTICS FIRST — for Google that means running the published version diff tool (developers.google.com/google-ads/api/diff-tool, including `common/metrics`) BEFORE the bump, not after; (2) NAME WHAT MOVES, in writing, before shipping; (3) EXPECT A SEAM in history at the cutover date and say where it is; (4) CHECK FOR ONE AFTERWARD against our own metrics_daily, the way LORAMER_META_SILENT_VERSION_UPGRADE_V1 did. **WE HAVE BEEN ON THE WRONG SIDE OF THIS TWICE, and both times the vendor was reporting the truth in a response header we were not reading** — Shopify (`x-shopify-api-version`: pin said 2025-01, served 2025-10) and Meta (`facebook-api-version`: sent v21.0, served v25.0). The remedy is already proven on one platform: `noteServedApiVersion` in shopifyGraphQL reads the served version on every response, dedupes per (host|sent|served) and console.errors a mismatch, because a mismatch means the pin is a claim rather than a fact. Every platform we integrate gets that instrument or we do not know what we are running. | LORAMER_VERSION_BUMP_IS_A_CAPTURE_EVENT_V1, 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

The file is NOT edited here (★META-ATTRIBUTION-COMMENT-FIX). WHAT REMAINS ON v25: 1d_click, 7d_click, 28d_click, 1d_view, plus a 1-day engage-through. **No long view window exists for anyone, on any plan, via any API.** `dda` is requested on every call and has never returned a row — status **UNVERIFIED** (removed vs never generally available vs gated; not chased). **(2) ON 2026-03-03 META REDEFINED CLICK-THROUGH TO COUNT GENUINE LINK CLICKS ONLY**, moving social clicks (likes, shares, saves, comments) into a new **engage-through** bucket — a definitional change to the three click windows we capture and store, described as a ramp with most accounts seeing full effect by July. **SEAM CHECK: NO SEAM DETECTABLE — AND THE INSTRUMENT COULD NOT HAVE DETECTED ONE.** Natural per-client variation in conversions-per-dollar is 2–6× per 28-day segment; a 20% definitional shift is an order of magnitude below that noise floor. The predicted mechanism (click windows down, 1d_view flat) is **not** confirmed and its inverse is not either — there is no coherent direction. ⛔ **WITHDRAWN:** my 2026-07-27 reading that the pooled "week of 03-02 = 1.92 vs week of 03-09 = 0.48" view/click discontinuity was "consistent with a definitional change" — it **did not survive per-client decomposition**; no individual client shows that transition. It was a cohort-composition artifact, the same trap as the pooled June series. **BANK THIS AS THE RULE, not just the result: absence of a detectable seam is NOT evidence of no seam.** It is evidence the data is too noisy and the cohort too small to find one of the size described. | probe + seam check 2026-07-27 | do not relitigate.

- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — LIVE PATH] **LORAMER_DEGRADED_IS_NOT_FAILED_V1 — a DEGRADED sub-fetch is a partial-capture warning, not a connection failure, and conflating them cost us three days.** THE DEFECT: `finalizeSection` in cron/sync computed `erroredConns = new Set(errsForP.map(e => e.clientId)).size` — every client with ANY entry in `summary.errors` counted as a failed connection. But cron/sync:660 pushes an entry for every DEGRADED sub-fetch, which by design never drops the connection (those family try/catches exist specifically so a partial failure never drops the platform's main rows or its sync_state write). MEASURED: the google 08:08:46Z run reported **17 attempted / 0 succeeded / 17 errored WHILE WRITING 77,647 ROWS**, three days running, because every client logged the same two degradations (audience, conversion_action). FIX: a `connFailed` set keyed `${platform}|${clientId}`, written ONLY by the five outer connection catches; errored counts from that set, degraded is the remainder, and degraded is SURFACED in the JSON summary and a console.warn — never hidden, because it is why conversion_action and audience are thin. Cross-ref LORAMER_INSTRUMENT_MUST_NAME_ITS_PHASE_V1 (an instrument can be correct and still mislead) and the narrow-green enforcers: this is the same family. FORWARD IS NOT STARVED.** Google forward runs at 08:08 UTC, completes by 08:19, and writes ~77,700 rows/day at **~1,044 GAQL operations across 18 clients — about 7% of the 15,000 Basic cap** — roughly three hours before the quota actually dies (the pause was written 11:31:59Z on 2026-07-27). **THE CONSUMING LANE IS CATCHUP.** It pays the SAME ~58-op per-client breadth fan-out **PER GAP DAY** across a ~35-day window, because it imports the identical DEVICE/GEO/USER_GEO/HOUR/DEMO grain sets and calls the per-Day fetchers once per missing day: **1,187,578 rows on 418 connection-attempts (2026-07-27) against forward's 77,696 on 18.** **THE RESERVE WORKS AS DESIGNED** — `googleForwardReserveDecision` is called from exactly one place, cron/drain:88, and holds the DRAIN off Google for 180 minutes after the reset until forward finishes; it was never built to hold back catchup, and that is not a defect in it. What WAS a defect, fixed this commit: catchup read the global quota pause ONLY in restore mode, so after exhaustion it kept firing every ten minutes until 11:59 (LORAMER_CATCHUP_QUOTA_PAUSE_V1 — the gate at cron/catchup:525 carried the same `restoreMode &&`, so both had to change). ⚠ **CONFIDENCE, stated because it bounds every number above: these are ARITHMETIC over grain-array lengths, not measurements. Past those bounds the set FREEZES — the same rows come back on every later call, forever. FOUR CONSEQUENCES, all LATENT not live (no client was at 500, no thread at 200), and the second is the one that matters most: (1) a HARD CONSTRAINT stated after row 500 never reaches the prompt — `extractDirectives` (build-claude-context:1079) scans the whole 500-row set, so the user states a binding rule, sees no error, and Lora never applies it; (2) the prompt block EXPLICITLY instructs her to deny memory gaps ("NEVER say 'each session is isolated' or 'I cannot see other tabs' — that is FALSE"), so past the bound that instruction is itself false and she would confidently narrate the wrong week rather than admit the gap — an omission the prompt converts into a confident assertion; (3) live turn context would be the BEGINNING of the thread, because the UI list hydrated by the capped GET is posted straight back as `history` (chat/route.ts:185); (4) answer recovery would report ABORTED_UNCONFIRMED on answers that WERE written, because `threadMaxIdRef` took row 200's id and the capped read can never return anything newer. FIX: both sorts reversed, and the array re-reversed to ascending immediately after the read (`conversationRows` in intelligence, the `messages:` return in conversations) so the truncation happens at the right end while EVERY downstream consumer — the surface:scope map, flattenConversations, extractDirectives, `flat.slice(-20)`, all four UI hydration sites, and the `slice(-8)` at dashboard:3744 — sees the order it already expected. The 500 and 200 bounds are UNCHANGED: this moves the window, it does not resize it. THE GENERAL RULE: **ORDER + LIMIT is one decision, not two. A sort direction chosen for rendering silently decides which rows survive truncation, and the two ends are opposite.** Gate-A 37/37 against the real compiled `buildClaudeContext` and `pickRecoveredAnswer`. src: 2026-07-27 diagnosis + fix.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — same commit, banked separately because it outlives the fix] **`created_at` IS NOT A UNIQUE KEY ON `client_conversations`, SO DESC + LIMIT WAS NON-DETERMINISTIC AT THE CUTOFF.** 92 rows share an exact `created_at` with another row in the same thread — every one stamped 2026-05-25, the legacy-blob migration. Without a tiebreaker a tie straddling the 500-row boundary is included or dropped at the planner's discretion, and the re-reversed order among ties is arbitrary. Both reads now order **`created_at DESC, id DESC`**; `id` is a bigserial, i.e. true insert order, and post-migration traffic is perfectly monotonic (measured: 0 id-vs-created_at disagreements across the 423 rows written since 2026-05-27), so the tiebreaker never fires on live rows. SIDE EFFECT, APPROVED BY RUSS 2026-07-27: **39 rows of May history now return in true insert order instead of Postgres heap order — same row set, corrected sequence.** Today's un-tiebroken query serves Escential's oldest rows as 13, 11, 19, 21, 45, 25…; with the tiebreaker, 11, 13, 19, 21, 25, 35… An id-ONLY sort was measured and REJECTED: it moves 94 rows, because the migration wrote historical `created_at` values that disagree with insert order. THE RULE: **a LIMIT on a non-unique sort key is a coin flip at the boundary — if you truncate on it, tiebreak it.** src: 2026-07-27, measured live.

- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — LIVE PATH + PROMPT] **LORAMER_GOOGLE_QUOTA_LORA_CAVEAT_V1 — the quota sentinel existed, was correct, carried the exact reset time, and NOTHING ON THE ANSWER PATH READ IT.** `sync_state/__google_quota` had exactly three readers, all in the backfill lanes (cron/drain:77, cron/catchup:266, google-forward-reserve:62). So during a ~21-hour exhaustion window (observed 2026-07-26 11:26Z, 2026-07-27 11:32Z, and again while this shipped) a user could ask a Google question and get one of three shapes, NONE naming the cause: total exhaustion rendered the honest-but-causeless "LIVE snapshot FAILED to load this turn (temporarily unavailable)" — so **Lora offered a retry that could not succeed for up to 21 hours**, which is a false promise dressed as helpfulness; the enriched-campaign fallback (google-intelligence:221) degraded status precision SILENTLY; and sub-family failures rendered per-family degradation with no hint that all twenty died of ONE platform-wide cause with a known recovery time. NOW: `/api/intelligence` reads the sentinel and the Google section carries a block naming the CAUSE and — the half only the sentinel can supply — the RESET TIME, which is what converts "unavailable" into something a user can act on. It explicitly forbids the retry offer, forbids $0 / no-spend / disconnected readings, and redirects to the captured store, which does not depend on the live quota. THREE PLACEMENT DECISIONS, each load-bearing: (1) the block sits ABOVE the `fetchFailed` early return in buildPlatformSection, because that branch RETURNS and total exhaustion is the shape the outage most often produces — placed anywhere below it, the caveat would be missing from precisely the worst case; (2) it is read BEFORE the cache check and re-attached on BOTH the cache-hit and cache-miss paths, because **a cached "all clear" outlives the outage and a cached "exhausted" outlives the recovery** — with a 15-minute TTL either lie persists past the moment it matters; (3) the status ladder's "temporarily unavailable" (build-claude-context:1297) became quota-aware in the same commit, because that line is read ABOVE the block and "temporarily" is simply false for 21 hours — unpaused it is byte-identical to what LORAMER_GOOGLE_CAMPAIGN_STATUS_FIX_V2 shipped. Both sites share ONE reset-time formatter so the prompt can never contradict itself about when data returns. Healthy quota ⇒ the field is undefined ⇒ the rendered prompt is BYTE-IDENTICAL to before, proven on both the populated and fetchFailed shapes. Gate-A 42/42 against the real compiled renderer plus the live sentinel. src: 2026-07-27 diagnosis + fix.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — same commit, the channel decision] **fetchErrors WAS THE WRONG CHANNEL FOR A PLATFORM-WIDE OUTAGE, AND THE REASON GENERALIZES.** The instruction was to reuse the LORAMER_LORA_FETCHERRORS_DEGRADED_V1 mechanism rather than invent a second one. The IDIOM was reused verbatim — NOT $0, NOT disconnected, redirect to the captured store, say it out loud — but the CHANNEL could not be: `fetchErrors` is PER-FAMILY and PER-FETCH, and on total exhaustion the entire platform object is replaced by `EMPTY_PLATFORM` at intelligence/route.ts:430, **which discards fetchErrors entirely**. A platform-wide, time-bounded outage with a known reset needs a channel that survives that substitution, so it rides its own optional field on ClientIntelligence. THE RULE: **reuse the idiom, not necessarily the pipe — check whether the pipe survives the failure you are describing.** A degradation channel that is thrown away by the very failure mode it is meant to report is not a channel. src: 2026-07-27.

If the quota dies during an on-demand turn (an /api/intelligence call rather than a drain fire), there is a sub-five-minute window in which the outage is real and the caveat is not yet armed; Lora falls back to the pre-existing honest-but-causeless message for that window. ACCEPTED, not fixed: the alternative is writing the sentinel from every GAQL caller, which multiplies the write sites for a five-minute improvement on a 21-hour problem. That is the difference between "ENABLED" and "ENABLED but not actually SERVING (payment / policy / still learning)", which she will state confidently and wrongly. NOW: one `fetchErrors.push({label:'campaign_status', message: describeGaqlError(enrichErr)})` in that catch — describeGaqlError (LORAMER_GAQL_ERROR_SERIALIZE_V1) because a google-ads rejection leaves `.message` empty and would otherwise land as "[object Object]". - [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — same commit, the shape decision, and it CORRECTS my own earlier scoping] **A DEGRADATION IS NOT AN ABSENCE, AND THE UNAVAILABLE SHAPE WOULD HAVE BEEN ITS OWN FALSE STATEMENT.** The plan (mine, approved) was to render campaign_status with the existing UNAVAILABLE-no-redirect prose. Implementing it exposed the flaw: that prose says "live fetch FAILED, and this family is LIVE-ONLY, so there is no fallback… offer to retry", but **the campaigns DID load, with REAL metrics, at coarser status precision**. It would have told Lora the campaigns were missing — false — and invited exactly the retry that LORAMER_GOOGLE_QUOTA_LORA_CAVEAT_V1 forbids during a quota outage. So FETCH_ERROR_FAMILIES now carries a THIRD shape via an optional `degradedNote`, and the taxonomy is explicit: **(1) MISSING-with-redirect** (captured elsewhere → query_breakdown / query_metrics), **(2) MISSING-no-redirect** (live-only, no captured equivalent — audiences, RSA assets, PMax), **(3) DEGRADED-with-real-data** (the family loaded at reduced precision; say what is still trustworthy AND what may no longer be asserted). The no-redirect ruling stands for campaign_status: serving-state has no captured equivalent, and pointing Lora at a tool that cannot answer would manufacture a second false zero. The shared block header was widened the same way — "either went MISSING FROM THIS PROMPT or loaded at REDUCED PRECISION" — but ONLY when such a family is present, so the ~35-36 existing degraded renders per week stay byte-identical. THE RULE: **when you add an honesty line, check that it is TRUE of the thing it describes; a wrong caveat spends the trust a right one buys.** src: 2026-07-27.
- [KNOWN EXCEPTION 2026-07-27 — stated, deliberately not smoothed] **IN CRON/CATCHUP THIS CAN NEWLY MARK A CONNECTION ERRORED, AND THAT IS A SECOND REASON ★CATCHUP-COUNTER-CONFLATION NEEDS ITS FLIGHT.** In cron/sync the new entry is correctly DEGRADED — the fetchErrors→summary.errors push does not touch `connFailed`, and today's real 17-connection shape still classifies 17 succeeded / 17 degraded / 0 errored with the campaign_status entry added (proven by executing the shipped expressions). In cron/catchup it is different, because catchup:315 still uses the conflated `new Set(errsForP.map(e => e.clientId)).size`. MOSTLY HARMLESS: catchup already pushed fetchErrors, so a client with any other degradation is already in that set and only the entry COUNT moves. **THE EXCEPTION, not asserted away: a client whose ONLY failure is campaign_status — enriched fails, base succeeds, every other sub-query succeeds — would newly enter catchup's errored set.** Rare during quota exhaustion, because when the quota is out the other sub-queries fail too and the client is already counted; but rare is not impossible, and it is recorded here rather than discovered later. src: 2026-07-27.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-27 — the guard gap I authored and closed in the same flight] **A GUARD THAT READS ONE RECORDING SHAPE PRINTS A GREEN THAT MEANS LESS THAN ITS NAME.** `fetch-errors-rendered.guard.mjs` extracted family labels ONLY from `safeQuery('<label>'`, so the moment a fetcher recorded via a DIRECT `fetchErrors.push` the guard was blind to it — it printed "18/18 families mapped" while blind to the site added minutes earlier, and an unmapped direct push would have rendered to Lora as a bare internal string with the guard still green. **This is the banked narrow-green failure mode, authored by me roughly an hour after banking the rule again** — which is the actual lesson: knowing the rule does not prevent the instance; only extending the instrument does. The extractor now matches both `safeQuery('<label>'` and `fetchErrors.push({ label: '<label>'`. PROVEN BY DELIBERATE FAILURE INJECTION, not by inspection: swapping the label to an unmapped `bogus_unmapped` makes the guard exit 1 with "UNMAPPED FAMILIES (1): bogus_unmapped"; restored, exit 0; the healthy run reads 19/19. src: 2026-07-27.
- [MEASURED 2026-07-27 — the expectation for tomorrow, so a moving number is not a mystery] **THIS ADDS +0 ERROR ENTRIES TO THE 08:08 FORWARD RUN.** The 7-day Vercel error cluster for the enriched fallback reads count=83 on routes **/api/cron/catchup and /api/intelligence** — it has NEVER fired on /api/cron/sync in the window, because the forward pass runs at 08:08 while the quota is healthy and the enriched query succeeds. - [SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — BACKEND-ISOLATED] **LORAMER_QUOTA_READ_SPLIT_STATE_V1 — the google-quota sentinel read has THREE outcomes, not two, and the two kinds of caller need OPPOSITE defaults when the answer is unknown.** THE DEFECT: `readGoogleQuotaPause` destructured only `data` and threw `error` away. supabase-js NEVER throws — it returns `{ data, error }` — so any read failure produced `data === null`, fell into `if (!data?.backfill_blocked)`, and returned `paused:false`. A FAILED READ WAS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A HEALTHY UNBLOCKED SENTINEL, silently, with no log line on that path. THE FIX: the function returns `state: 'blocked' | 'not_blocked' | 'unknown'` beside `paused`; `paused` keeps its EXACT prior meaning (so the answer path needed no edit), an error returns `paused:false + state:'unknown'` and console.errors it as a DB READ FAILURE explicitly labelled not-a-quota-block, and the clock-based auto-resume branch is unchanged in position and comparison. **CAPTURE LANES HOLD ON UNKNOWN; THE ANSWER PATH STAYS SILENT ON UNKNOWN.** `holdGoogleWork(qp)` = `paused || state==='unknown'` is THE SINGLE PLACE that capture-lane rule lives — cron/drain:77 and cron/catchup:266 gate on it, and no lane may hand-roll the disjunction. /api/intelligence:220 deliberately stays on `.paused`. GUARD: `tests/guards/google-quota-read-fails-open.guard.mjs`, wired into `npm run guard`, hermetic, EXECUTES the real reader AND the real renderers (buildGoogleQuotaLines + buildClaudeContextCacheable); SEEN RED pre-fix (2/6) and mutation-proven in all three directions (fail-open reverted → RED; elapsed-window broken → RED; answer path flipped closed → source pin RED). ⚠ **WHAT IT DOES NOT CLAIM: this makes the fail-open impossible and the failure LOUD; it does NOT prove the fail-open is what fired on 2026-07-28 — that path logged nothing, so the evidence never existed.** See QUEUE ★CATCHUP-PAUSE-NOT-BITING for the observation that settles it. | LORAMER_QUOTA_READ_SPLIT_STATE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [LAW COLLISION 2026-07-28 — RESOLVED, and written down precisely so nobody "simplifies" it back to a boolean] **FAIL-CLOSED EVERYWHERE WOULD HAVE BROKEN A DO-NOT-RELITIGATE LAW, AND THE SPLIT IS WHAT LETS BOTH STAND.** The obvious fix — error → `paused:true` — is correct for the capture lanes and WRONG for the answer path, because `readGoogleQuotaPause` has two kinds of caller: cron/drain and cron/catchup turn the answer into "do I spend Google quota" (a false hold costs one lap, retried in ~10 min, self-healing), while /api/intelligence turns it into a USER-FACING CLAIM — buildGoogleQuotaLines renders "⛔ GOOGLE PLATFORM API QUOTA EXHAUSTED … SAY THIS OUT LOUD" plus an instruction not to offer a retry. Under fail-closed with a null row that block renders with `until` unset, so Lora would tell a user the quota is exhausted until "the next daily reset" when the only thing that happened is a Supabase blip and live Google data is fine. That directly violates **FAIL-PARTIAL READ-PATH LAW** (064dd54, 2026-07-11: read-path endpoints fail PARTIAL + LOUD, never fail-closed-silent) and re-creates the exact incident **VERIFICATION LAW 1** was written from ("Lora: /api/intelligence fail-closed on a network blip"). It also collides with LORA-SEES-EVERYTHING: telling a user their own data is unavailable because OUR read failed is the named failure mode, not a boundary. ALSO CHECKED AND STATED: the `reason` string cannot soften it — both render sites (build-claude-context.ts:538 block, :1340 status ladder) key off `paused` and `until` only and never read `reason`, so a reason naming the read failure would be invisible to Lora and the user alike. THE RULE THAT OUTLIVES THIS: **when one predicate serves both a work-gate and a user-facing claim, it needs two answers, not one default — pick the safe direction PER CALLER, and put the caller rule in one named function so the next lane inherits it instead of re-deciding it.** | LORAMER_QUOTA_READ_SPLIT_STATE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — BACKEND READ-PATH] **LORAMER_SCOPED_DRILLDOWN_FALSE_ZERO_V1 — a scope that cannot reach the data is NOT the data being absent, and the THIRD instance of a class we have now fixed three times.** THE DEFECT, measured: `queryBreakdown` resolved the entity level by probing COARSEST-FIRST and IGNORING `parentEntityId`/`entityId`, then applied the scope filter at that level. Families whose coarsest level happened to match the scope worked (keyword/search_term coarsest=ad_group → 45 and 182 values returned; hour coarsest=campaign → 24). `device` (coarsest=campaign) could NEVER match an ad-group entityId, and campaign-level rows carry `parent_entity_id` = the CUSTOMER id, so a campaign parentEntityId missed too — returning rows=0 plus **"No device data captured for this client in <window>"** while $63,992.80 of device data sat one level away. **THE RULE THIS INHERITS, and the reason it is banked as a CLASS: ABSENCE, FAILURE and ZERO ARE THREE DIFFERENT FACTS, and the caller must be able to tell them apart.** PRIOR INSTANCES, named so the FOURTH is found faster: (1) **LORAMER_WOO_SILENT_ZERO_FIX_V1** (2026-07-25) — a thrown fetch returned a zero-filled SUCCESS object, so forward wrote $0 rows for days the store had sales and eleven breadth families were silently skipped; (2) **query_metrics FALSE-ZERO on pre-data windows** — 0 rows before data start rendered as "$0" instead of "no data". Same disease, three layers. THE FIX, in the preference order Russ set: the level probe now runs WITH the scope predicates applied, so the level is resolved FROM THE SCOPE rather than from coarseness; unscoped calls are byte-identical (the scope-less probe IS the original). When a scope genuinely matches nothing, the query re-probes UNSCOPED and — if the family exists — returns an HONEST SCOPE-LIMITATION note naming the levels where it IS available, plus additive `scopeUnavailable` / `availableEntityLevels` fields, explicitly instructing the reader not to report zero. The old "No <bt> data captured" sentence now fires ONLY when the family truly has no rows at any level. MEASURED AFTER: device unscoped Σ$81,962.98 · scoped to campaign Σ$25,012.53 · scoped to ad group Σ$18,571.83 · scoped to a bogus id → 0 rows + the honest note — i.e. the scope DISCRIMINATES and is not silently ignored. GUARD: `scripts/check-scoped-drilldown.mjs`, 8/8, DB-dependent so deliberately OUT of `npm run guard`/`build` (same posture as check:data). Seen RED pre-fix (2/8, both core) and mutation-proven three ways — probe reverted to ignore scope → RED; honest-note branch forced off → RED; `scopeUnavailable` dropped while keeping the note → RED (a partial fix must not pass). | LORAMER_SCOPED_DRILLDOWN_FALSE_ZERO_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-28 — the non-regression that had to be proven, not assumed] **THE LEVEL PROBE NOW RESOLVES FROM SCOPE, NOT COARSENESS — AND THAT DOES NOT REGRESS LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_LEVEL_SCOPE_V1.** The 2026-07-02 fix exists because `query_breakdown` used to SUM every captured level and DOUBLE-COUNT (google hour $3,945.88 → true $2,427.36; the -next age card ~4x inflated). The obvious way to "make scoped drill-down find the rows" is to widen the level set until something matches — **that would trade a false zero for a false total, which is strictly worse**, because a wrong number is reported with the same confidence as a right one and nothing downstream can tell. THIS CHANGE MOVES *WHICH* SINGLE LEVEL IS CHOSEN; IT NEVER WIDENS THE SET TO MORE THAN ONE. Proven, not asserted: the guard carries a standing assertion that the unscoped google `hour` total lands on the ONE-LEVEL figure (~$81,962.98) and FAILS above $120,000, which is what campaign+ad_group summed would produce. src: 2026-07-28. | do not relitigate.
- [TEST-INFRA CORRECTION 2026-07-28 — recorded because the instrument was wrong and it nearly became a verdict about the model] **THE L10 EVAL QUESTION SCORED LORA AGAINST A NUMBER THE TOOL COULD NOT SERVE.** The L2 harness computed step 5's ground truth ("top device in that ad group" = MOBILE $13,520.48) straight from `metrics_daily` at ad_group level — a row that EXISTS, but that `queryBreakdown` could not return through any argument combination before this fix. So part of that question was unanswerable by construction, and the failing grade was partly an artefact of the harness, not of the answer. **THE RULE: TEST INFRASTRUCTURE CAN BE WRONG, AND A FAILING EVAL QUESTION IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY A LORA FAILURE — establish that the answer is REACHABLE THROUGH THE REAL PATH before scoring it.** This is the same family as VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT-BEFORE-TRUSTING-THE-MEASUREMENT, applied to the eval set itself; the same harness had already been caught truncating ground truth at PostgREST's 1000-row ceiling the day it was written. Note what Lora actually did on L10: she opened with "The chain breaks at step 1 — and that invalidates steps 2-6 as asked", named the Google quota outage as the cause, and refused to present figures she could not stand behind. - [DECIDED 2026-07-28 — RUSS] **MULTI-ACCOUNT = BOTH. ROLLUP IS THE DEFAULT; PER-ACCOUNT BREAKOUT IS AVAILABLE ON REQUEST.** Closes the open decision at LORAMER_QUEUE_OF_RECORD.md ("MULTI-ACCOUNT rollup-vs-breakout DECISION is OPEN — RUSS'S CALL"), which the 2026-06-05 ROADMAP entry posed and did not resolve. **THE RULE: an all-time or cross-period question SUMS across every account the client holds on that platform and the seam is INVISIBLE — the user must never have to know an account boundary exists in order to get a correct total.** A per-account BREAKOUT is produced when asked for ("which account drove that", "show me each account"), never as the default shape. **DRIVING CASE — Foam OH (957d484e), and the wording matters because a premise was corrected on measurement: the legacy Google account 7688521852 is DORMANT, NOT dead.** It is still CONNECTED (platform_connections row created 2026-05-14, owner cotebrandmarketing@gmail.com, google_tokens row present and refreshing) and the cron is still capturing it daily through 2026-07-27; it simply has no spend since 2026-04-05. **AND THAT IS THE CONCRETE BLOCKER: the dormant account OCCUPIES THE SINGLE `UNIQUE(client_id, platform)` SLOT on platform_connections, so the NEW account cannot be connected at all until that constraint widens** (QUEUE ★MULTIACCOUNT-SLOT-BLOCKER). The clean-break premise otherwise HOLDS on the spend timeline — last spend 2026-04-05, nothing since, no time overlap — so the rollup is a CONCATENATION rather than a merge and the default answer is simply correct. | multi-account decision, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
- [HAZARD 2026-07-28 — banked with the decision, and it is THREE states, not two] **INSIDE A ROLLUP, ABSENCE AND ZERO MUST STAY DISTINGUISHABLE PER ACCOUNT — AND "ABSENCE" ITSELF SPLITS IN TWO.** The three states a rollup must never collapse: **(a) GENUINELY ZERO** — captured, a real $0 day (Foam OH 2026-06 and 2026-07 are exactly this: 29 and 27 captured days at $0.00); **(b) ABSENT-RECOVERABLE** — not captured, still inside the platform's retention window, so it can be backfilled (Foam OH 2026-05, an entire missing month); **(c) ABSENT-PERMANENT** — not captured and past retention, unrecoverable for all time (Foam OH's ~101 missing days across 2022–2023, all beyond Google's ~37-month floor). Summed blindly, all three read as zero and the rollup reports a confident wrong total. **A PER-ACCOUNT CAPTURE FLOOR IS NECESSARY BUT NOT SUFFICIENT: a floor alone can separate "before we started" from "after we started", but it CANNOT tell (b) from (c)** — both are gaps after the floor, and only the platform's retention boundary distinguishes them. Today the floor is scoped per (client, platform), so a rollup starting at the OLD account's floor would silently treat the NEW account's pre-capture days as real zeros. SAME CLASS as the false-zero fixes shipped 2026-07-28 (LORAMER_SCOPED_DRILLDOWN_FALSE_ZERO_V1 — a scope that cannot reach the data is not the data being absent) and 2026-07-25 (LORAMER_WOO_SILENT_ZERO_FIX_V1 — a thrown fetch is not a $0 day), plus the query_metrics pre-data-window fix: ABSENCE, FAILURE and ZERO are different facts, and this decision adds a fourth distinction inside ABSENCE itself. | multi-account hazard, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.

- [DECIDED 2026-07-28 — the design choice, banked so it is not "simplified" into a shared cursor later] **THE DIRECT ASSET ROUTE IS STATELESS: NO CURSOR, NO sync_state WRITE.** Sharing the drain's `meta_asset` cursor was considered and REJECTED. `rangeLap` (drain-registry.ts:189-205) does an UNGUARDED read-modify-write on `backfill_earliest_date`, and the `__drain_meta` claim leases the CONNECTION for drain laps only — it would not exclude a route. Two writers on one cursor is a lost-update race in BOTH directions: the route could rewind the drain's frontier, or a drain lap could rewind the route's. Going stateless deletes the shared mutable state rather than guarding it, and it is the only race-free option that does not modify the drain. **BOTH SIBLINGS ALREADY DO THIS DELIBERATELY** — meta-video's header says it outright: "NO cursor / NO sync_state — the drain's rangeLap owns the 'meta_video' cursor". THE ACCEPTED COST, stated rather than discovered later: the drain's cursor does not learn what the route captured, so the drain RE-WALKS covered ground. That is WASTEFUL, NOT WRONG — every write is an idempotent upsert on METRICS_DAILY_CONFLICT, so a re-walk rewrites byte-identical rows, and a concurrent drain lap on the same client is therefore safe. Reconciling the cursor afterwards is a separate decision, deliberately NOT made here. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_DIRECT_ROUTE_V1, 2026-07-28 | do not relitigate.
