- [SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — ADDITIVE BACKEND] **LORAMER_META_ASSET_BUDGET_HEADROOM_V1 — a between-iteration budget check that reserves nothing for the NEXT iteration is not a budget, and it cost two live 504s.** THE DEFECT, in code I wrote the same day: `/api/backfill/meta-asset` checked only `elapsed > BUDGET_MS` between sub-ranges. At t=249s under a 250s budget it PASSED that check, began a fresh 33-report calendar month, and ran past Vercel's `maxDuration = 300` — so the lambda was KILLED and the caller never received `resumeBefore`. **The chaining contract, which is the only thing that makes the route resumable in practice, could not work at all**: every invocation on a heavy client 504s, and no client-side timeout can fix it (the second attempt used --max-time 400 precisely to rule that out, and still took a 504 at 300.34s). **WHY IT DID NOT SURFACE ON THE SIBLINGS I COPIED:** /api/backfill/meta-video runs ~4 reports per lap and meta-placement is similar; assets run 33 (11 breakdowns × 3 entity levels). The same check is safe at 4 and unsafe at 33 — the pattern was inherited without its assumption. THE FIX: measure each lap's duration as it completes, and start another lap only if the remaining budget covers the WORST lap observed so far, or `FIRST_LAP_MS` (90s, deliberately generous) before anything is measured. Extracted as a PURE function `shouldStartAnotherLap` in the new `src/lib/backfill/lap-budget.ts`, so it is provable without a server and without a live Meta call (a Next route file may not export arbitrary symbols). The response now also returns `lapsRun`, `maxLapMs`, `lapMs[]` and `reportsIssued`, because across two live runs the report count was never once observable. ⛔ **THE RULE, which outlives this route: A BETWEEN-ITERATION BUDGET CHECK IS ONLY SAFE IF ONE ITERATION CANNOT EXCEED THE REMAINING CEILING. If an iteration can be long, RESERVE HEADROOM for it — measured from the iterations already run, with a conservative default before anything is measured. Lowering the constant does not fix this; it only changes the size of lap that still overruns.** GATE-A 8/8 pure-function cases incl. the exact observed 504 shape (t=249s, 60s lap → must break); mutation-tested by reverting to the bare `elapsed <= budget` → 4/8 RED, restored byte-exact. NOTHING ELSE TOUCHED: maxDuration unchanged, the drain, drain-registry.ts and meta-asset-backfill.ts byte-identical. ⚠ KNOWN BLIND SPOT, stated not worked around: Meta's `X-Business-Use-Case-Usage` is consumed inside runMetaAssetBackfill and never returned, so this route CANNOT surface throttle status without changing the writer — which was explicitly out of scope. Throttle remains observable only via a writer error or the Vercel error clusters, and after two live runs it is still UNKNOWN. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_BUDGET_HEADROOM_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.
- [SHIPPED 2026-07-29 — SHARED WRITER] **LORAMER_META_ASSET_CODEPOINT_TRUNCATION_V1 — the corruption was OURS, not Meta's: we created an invalid UTF-8 sequence at truncation and Postgres rejected the whole upsert.** THE DEFECT: `meta-asset-backfill.ts:35` capped `breakdown_value` with `String.slice(0, VALUE_CAP)`. JS strings are UTF-16, so `.slice()` cuts by CODE UNIT, not codepoint. Foam OH's body_asset for campaign/2024-11-01 is 307 chars ending "…🟠🟡🟢"; the cut at index 299 landed INSIDE U+1F7E2 (surrogate pair D83D DFE2), keeping the lone high surrogate. JS passes that through `JSON.stringify` unharmed; the UTF-8 encoding on the wire renders it EF BF BD and Postgres rejects the statement with `invalid input syntax for type json` — halting the lap and every family ordered behind body_asset. **VERIFIED THE SOURCE DATA WAS CLEAN: all 53 rows Meta returned for that day were scanned for NUL, lone surrogates and control characters — zero found.** The first hypothesis (a NUL byte in ad copy) was measured and KILLED before any fix was written. ⛔ **THE RULE: ANY CAP APPLIED TO EXTERNAL TEXT MUST CUT BY CODEPOINT.** `[...str].slice(0, cap).join('')` iterates codepoints and can never split a pair; `.slice()` splits silently, with no error and no warning, and the damage surfaces only at a boundary that validates UTF-8. Emoji in ad copy is normal marketing text, not an edge case. FIX: new `src/lib/backfill/safe-truncate.ts` — `capText()` cuts by codepoint, then strips any lone surrogate that was already present, and REPORTS what it changed (`value_truncated` / `value_sanitised` land in `extra`, so sanitisation is never invisible). Wired at meta-asset-backfill.ts:117 and :130. BLAST RADIUS: this writer has two callers — drain-registry.ts:30 (the `meta_asset` step, 4 fires/day across 13 Meta clients) and /api/backfill/meta-asset — so a regression breaks scheduled Meta capture for every client AND the direct route simultaneously. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_CODEPOINT_TRUNCATION_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.
- [GUARD LESSON 2026-07-29 — it cost two mutation rounds to find and it generalises] **A SANITISER DOWNSTREAM OF A DEFECT CAN MASK THAT DEFECT IN TESTS. ASSERT PRESERVATION, NOT JUST VALIDITY.** The first version of `scripts/check-asset-text-cap.mjs` asserted only that capped text contained no lone surrogate and re-encoded as valid UTF-8. It went GREEN under mutation — reverting the codepoint slice to a raw `.slice()` still produced VALID output, because `stripLoneSurrogates` ran afterwards and removed the orphan. The guard could not distinguish "correctly truncated" from "silently dropped the character", **and silent loss is the WORSE failure**: the mutant turns a loud Postgres rejection into quiet data loss that no check would ever surface. FIX: assert the emoji SURVIVES — `value.endsWith('\u{1F7E2}')` and exactly 298 codepoints. Under mutation that now reads `cp=297 endsWith🟢=false valid=true`, i.e. valid AND wrong, which is precisely the state the weak assertion could not see. ⚠ ALSO BANKED, because it nearly shipped a vacuous green: my FIRST mutation attempt used a regex that did not match, so the file was never modified and the guard "passed" against unmutated code. **A MUTATION TEST THAT DOES NOT MUTATE IS WORTH NOTHING — anchor-check that the edit applied before trusting the result.** Same family as VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT-BEFORE-TRUSTING-THE-MEASUREMENT, applied to the guard rather than the data. | LORAMER_META_ASSET_CODEPOINT_TRUNCATION_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.

- [LAW 2026-07-29 — earned tonight, and it saved a day of wasted building] **PROBE BEFORE BUILDING: PRESENCE IN A WRITER'S REGISTRY MEANS WE *REQUEST* A BREAKDOWN, NOT THAT THE VENDOR *RETURNS* IT.** I confirmed three asset-type dims were in ASSET_BREAKDOWNS and inferred from that they would backfill. The check was right; the inference was wrong — a full clean run fetched them for 23 months and wrote nothing. **ZERO ROWS IS AMBIGUOUS: it can mean nobody fetched, or that nothing exists to fetch, and those demand opposite responses** — one is a route to build, the other is a dead end to record. - [DECIDED 2026-07-29 — SHARED WRITER] **LORAMER_METRICS_UPSERT_CHUNKED_V1 — ONE shared chunked writer for metrics_daily, because a single `.upsert(array)` is a single Postgres statement and on an already-populated slice every row in it is an UPDATE.** THE DEFECT: `google-geo-backfill.ts:77` issued one statement per (grain × entity_level × day). MEASURED payloads: **15,587 rows** (Foam OH `geo_city`/campaign 2026-03-25) and **13,117 rows** (Inside 2025-07-24). Those slices were already populated, so every row was a CONFLICT — an UPDATE, not an insert — and the published measurement of that shape is an overwrite batch costing **~281× the same batch as pure inserts** (Datadog, "When upserts don't update but still write"). metrics_daily carries SIX indexes totalling ~14 GB against a ~16 GB heap, including the **8.2 GB unique conflict-key index** every row must probe; `spend`/`revenue` are INCLUDE columns on `idx_metrics_daily_account_canonical`, so an update touching them defeats HOT and forces full index maintenance per row. ⛔ **IT IS NOT A MISSING INDEX — do not re-diagnose it as one.** EXPLAIN on the exact conflict predicate is a clean `Index Only Scan using idx_metrics_daily_client_platform_bt_level_date`, cost 290; migration 024 (LORAMER_BREAKDOWN_INDEX_V1) is doing its job. ⛔ **AND THE REMEDY IS NOT A HIGHER CEILING** — reduce the batch; raising `statement_timeout` is separately forbidden (the LIVE limit is 8s via the PostgREST `authenticator` role). **THE RATCHET, which is why four weeks of drain fires could not heal it:** `runGeoFamily` returns 500 on the FIRST failing day, so `rangeLap` never advances `backfill_earliest_date` — both geo cursors sat frozen since 2026-06-28, retrying the SAME 40-day window every fire — and earlier partial successes had already turned the inserts into updates, so **every retry was strictly more expensive than the one before it**. A failure mode that gets harder each time it is attempted cannot self-heal and must never be waited out. THE FIX: `src/lib/metrics-upsert.ts` → `upsertMetricsChunked(rows, {chunkSize=1000})`, which slices the payload, owns the 7-col conflict key, and calls `normalizeMetricsRows` **internally** so no caller can skip the union-of-keys guard (LORAMER_SHOPIFY_DEPTH_NOTNULL_FIX_V1); it throws naming the chunk index, chunk size and rows-already-written, so a future timeout is tuned from evidence. FLIGHT 1 rerouted exactly ONE call site (google-geo); the other 52 are ★METRICS-UPSERT-FLIGHT-2. ⚠ HONEST LIMIT, stated not implied away: the chunk size bounds ROW COUNT, not statement COST, and it is **NOT proven against the 8s ceiling** — Gate-A (d), the live lap, was blocked by an exhausted Google quota and is OPEN. | LORAMER_METRICS_UPSERT_CHUNKED_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.
- [FINDING 2026-07-29 — METHOD, and it invalidates a whole class of past inventories] **A SINGLE-LINE GREP OF THIS REPO UNDERCOUNTS metrics_daily WRITE SITES BY HALF: 25 found vs 53 real.** The diagnosis grepped `.from('metrics_daily').upsert(` on one line and found 25 call sites. The guard, which scans a **4-line window** because this codebase habitually splits `.from('metrics_daily')` and `.upsert(` across lines, found **53** at HEAD. The first allowlist I wrote was therefore built on a number that was wrong by 28 sites. ⛔ **THE RULE: any inventory of this repo built from a single-line grep is UNVERIFIED.** Derive it by executing a scan with a multi-line window, and have the checker produce its own inventory rather than trusting a hand-written list — the same generate-don't-guard principle already banked for BREAKDOWN_REGISTRY and the resume digest. SECOND HALF OF THE SAME LESSON, caught in the same hour: my first allowlist pinned **file:line** and was built from a DIRTY working tree, so it read GREEN locally and would have gone RED on Vercel against the very commit it shipped in — the uncommitted Google widen moves every upsert below it in cron/sync. Re-keyed to **file + COUNT**: immune to line drift, and the anti-rot property survives (observed must EQUAL listed, so paying a file's debt FAILS until the ledger is dropped). | LORAMER_METRICS_UPSERT_CHUNKED_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.
- [DECIDED 2026-07-29 — TEST TOOLING] **LORAMER_GUARD_RUNALL_V1 — the guard chain stopped at the first failure and hid every guard behind it; it now RUNS ALL, COLLECTS, and REPORTS ONCE.** THE DEFECT: `npm run guard` was **24 segments joined by `&&`**, so the shell stopped at the first non-zero exit and every later guard was never invoked. A RED build therefore reported ONE finding and concealed an unknown number of others. ⛔ **AND THE HIDDEN HALF IS THE DANGEROUS HALF: a green tail that NEVER EXECUTED is indistinguishable, in the output, from a green tail that PASSED.** That is the narrow-green class this repo has banked repeatedly — "a green check answers a narrower question than the reader assumes" — turned inward on the guards themselves, which is the one place it had never been looked for. **HOW IT WAS FOUND, and the provenance matters: not by tooling.** On 2026-07-29 a failure at segment 13 (metrics-upsert-chunked) meant guards 14-24 never ran, and the only reason "no third failure" could be asserted at all is that a human ran the remaining ELEVEN BY HAND. Nothing in the pipeline said they had been skipped; the report would otherwise have claimed a clean chain on evidence that did not exist. THE FIX: `scripts/run-guards.mjs` — ordered list of all 24 (diff-proven identical to the old chain, same order, none dropped), spawns each, continues past failures, prints a SCOREBOARD first and then each failure's OWN stdout/stderr VERBATIM (never summarised — the allowlist tables and payload-shape dumps ARE the evidence), exits non-zero if anything failed or crashed. ⛔ **THREE BUCKETS, NOT TWO, and the third is the point: PASSED / FAILED / CRASHED.** A guard that RAN and failed its assertion is a FINDING; a guard that could not run at all — missing file, bad import, syntax error, signal — is a **BROKEN INSTRUMENT**, which is neither evidence the code is fine nor evidence it is broken, but evidence that we currently cannot tell. **VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT APPLIES TO THE GUARDS THEMSELVES**, and a runner that folded a crash into the failure count would be laundering an unknown as a known. CRASHED is printed FIRST, before findings, because it bounds what the whole run may claim. ALSO: an `UNRELIABLE` marking — if breakdown-registry-drift fails, check-capture-completeness and check-lora-grounding still RUN but are never printed as a bare PASS, because both reason over registry-derived sets and their green would describe a stale artifact; resume-digest-freshness is ORDER-LOCKED LAST (asserted in code) because anywhere else it produces a false RED before the wrap has stamped. **ADAPT-VS-AUTHOR, decided against the published option and stated (WEB-FIRST law):** npm-run-all2's `--continue-on-error` is the maintained implementation of run-all-collect-exit-nonzero and was REJECTED for three concrete reasons — it orchestrates NPM SCRIPTS not commands (adopting it means inventing 24 script entries whose only purpose is to be named), it treats every non-zero exit identically so it cannot give the FAILED-vs-CRASHED split that is the entire point, and it would be a NEW DEPENDENCY ON THE BUILD PATH (`npm run build` → `npm run guard`, executed on Vercel) — the same call as the Vercel-AI-SDK rejection in LORAMER_CHAT_STREAMING_V1. The PATTERN is theirs; the script is ours because those three requirements are not in it. GATE-A: tsc 0; run on the dirty tree = 24 ran, 23 passed, 1 failed, 0 crashed, with all eleven previously-skipped guards NAMED in the passed list; **CRASHED bucket SEEN — one list entry pointed at a nonexistent path exited with code 1, the SAME code an assertion failure uses, and was still classified CRASHED off the module-load signature rather than mislabelled FAILED**; runner restored byte-identical (sha256 match) after the mutation. ⚠ HONEST LIMIT, named not sold: Node exits 1 both for an uncaught throw and for our guards' assertion failures, so the split is a stderr-SHAPE heuristic (module-load errors, or an Error line plus a stack frame) rather than a proof. A guard whose own failure text printed a stack-shaped string would be mislabelled CRASHED — the SAFE direction, since it over-reports a broken instrument rather than hiding one, but a heuristic all the same. | LORAMER_GUARD_RUNALL_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.

VERDICT: (b) DISTINCT SLICES → SUM.** THE EVIDENCE, one read-only Insights call, act_1993803118152438 (The Escential Group), 2026-04-29, level=campaign, breakdowns=product_id: **103 rows, 102 distinct conflict keys, ONE key with two rows** — campaign `6896654646797`, value `"43340981010520, Amber Musk Fragrance"`, carrying **spend 0.08 / impressions 4 / clicks 0** and **spend 4.93 / impressions 106 / clicks 5**. ⛔ **THE METRICS DIFFER, SO IT IS NOT A PAGER ARTIFACT** — a pagination repeat is byte-identical, and `paging.next` was absent (one page, 103 rows against limit=500). Meta is grouping on a dimension it does not return in this field set. Last-wins would have kept 4.93 and silently discarded 0.08 — an understatement that would look entirely plausible. **BOTH BRANCHES SHIP, because the two mechanisms demand OPPOSITE fixes and the code can tell them apart without knowing which occurred:** rows sharing a key whose metrics are BYTE-IDENTICAL are a **verbatim repeat → COLLAPSE to one** (summing there would DOUBLE-COUNT, and `meta-graph-paged.ts` genuinely has no de-dup); rows whose metrics DIFFER are **distinct slices → SUM** spend/impressions/clicks/conversions/conversion_value/revenue. ⛔ **RATIOS ARE RECOMPUTED, NEVER SUMMED.** `extra.ctr/cpc/cpm` are re-derived from the MERGED totals through the same `ratio()` the builder uses: summing the two input CTRs gives **4.717**, the correct recomputation gives **4.5455** — a summed ratio is a wrong number that looks right, which is the whole reason it is asserted separately in the guard. Non-additive, non-derivable fields are taken from the first row of the group and that is SAFE rather than a guess: every one of them is either IN the key, a pure function of `entity_id` (which is in the key), or a per-run constant off FieldCfg. **PLACEMENT, decided and not to be re-opened: the merge lives in `meta-simple-breakdown-core.ts`, where the column semantics are known — NOT in `upsertMetricsChunked`.** That writer stays SEMANTICS-FREE precisely so its every-platform blast radius stays behaviour-neutral; a type-aware summing merge inside the shared chunker would silently change what a duplicate key MEANS for every platform, including families where two rows sharing a key might legitimately be something else. Wired ONCE, after `byDate` is built and BEFORE anything counts, samples, reconciles or writes, so both upsert sites cannot diverge; `bucket.spend` is recomputed from merged rows so a verbatim collapse cannot leave the reconcile anchor double-counted (load-bearing for the two ANCHORED families on this engine, comscore_market and frequency_value — product_id is anchor:'none'). Every merge logs LOUD and is surfaced as `dedupeMerges` + `dedupe[]` in the lap body — a log line expires in an hour, the lap report does not. - [FINDING 2026-07-29 — METHOD, and it invalidates a whole class of audit] **THE STORED-DATA TAUTOLOGY: a write-REJECTION class can never be found by querying the store.** Postgres REJECTS a multi-row `INSERT … ON CONFLICT` that contains a duplicate key — it does not silently collapse it. So a duplicate conflict key can NEVER appear in `metrics_daily`; every stored day is duplicate-free BY CONSTRUCTION. **"I queried for duplicates and found zero" is therefore NOT evidence of absence — it is a restatement of how the database works**, and I ran exactly that query before realising it could only ever return zero. (Worse, my first pass grouped without `entity_id` and produced ten apparent "duplicates" that were the same product on different ads — legitimate, and caught only by re-reading the conflict key.) ⛔ **THE RULE: to audit a class whose symptom is a REJECTED WRITE, read the VENDOR PAYLOAD, not the store.** The only visible symptom in our own data was whole MISSING DAYS behind a generic 500 — invisible to every completeness check we own, because those check what IS stored. Same family as VERIFY-THE-INSTRUMENT and as the narrow-green enforcers: the instrument was answering a different question than the one asked. **COROLLARY, and it closes an open suspicion rather than leaving it hanging: the measured 49% product_id shortfall ($7,128.70 of $13,889.16, FoamOh 2024-11-28..29) is FULLY explained by non-partitioning and cannot include dropped duplicates** — the days that shortfall was measured on WROTE SUCCESSFULLY, which proves they contained no duplicate keys at all. Nothing was dropped, because nothing could have been. | LORAMER_META_BREAKDOWN_DEDUPE_V1, 2026-07-29 | do not relitigate.

- [FINDING 2026-07-29 — TOOLING, the same defect not re-introduced one script downstream] **`npm run check:data` IS CHAINED WITHOUT `&&` ACROSS ALL THREE CHECKS: `node A; A=$?; node B; B=$?; node C; C=$?; exit <max>`.** All three ALWAYS run and the WORST exit code wins, so a config error's exit 2 is not flattened into a guard failure's 1. An `&&` chain would mean an account-row failure silently prevents the frozen-cursor and identity checks from running at all — and you would read "one failure" as "one problem". That is the exact short-circuit defect found in `npm run guard` earlier today (LORAMER_GUARD_RUNALL_V1); having just paid to fix it upstream, re-introducing it downstream would have been the same bug with a different name. PROVEN, not assumed: with a deliberately failing first check, the later checks still ran and the chain exited non-zero. | package.json check:data | do not relitigate.

BASELINING AN UNEXPLAINED FREEZE ON A COHORT CLIENT IS NOT.** THE DISCRIMINATOR IS TWO QUESTIONS, BOTH OF WHICH MUST BE ANSWERED YES: **is the cause NAMED**, and **does anything real depend on the data**. - [LAW 2026-07-31 — GOVERNING] **LORAMER_GOOGLE_OP_BUDGET_V1 — ALLOCATE BEFORE SPENDING. A LANE MUST KNOW ITS SHARE BEFORE IT SPENDS, NOT AFTER THE CAP IS GONE.** **WHAT WE HAD WAS STOP-WHEN-DEAD.** `holdGoogleWork` is REACTIVE — it reads a sentinel GOOGLE set AFTER the 15k Basic-Access cap was already exhausted, so it structurally cannot stop the lane that spent it. This is both — a per-day ceiling AND a reserve the low-priority lane may not touch. **THE SPLIT:** cap 15,000 · CATCHUP_ALLOCATION 4,500 (30%, ≈67 gap-days/day) · RANKED_RESERVE 10,500 for forward, the geo lap and scoped recovery. The standing remedy — *check the Vercel logs before concluding* — is a HUMAN INSTRUCTION, and human instructions are exactly what failed four separate times on 2026-07-30 (1,223 GA days absent, a dead credential behind an HTTP 200, twelve 401s recorded as skips, ten days of search-term absence on a spending client). Free-tier Vercel logs also expire in ONE HOUR, so the instruction is frequently impossible to follow even when remembered. **THE SHAPE:** `capture_pass_log` (migration 049) — ONE ROW PER WRITER INVOCATION, written on EVERY exit path including nothing-observed, skipped and thrown. It records the DENOMINATOR (entities_examined, facts_examined) beside the numerator (rows_opened/closed/touched). Zero output rows PLUS a pass row = nothing changed; NO pass row = nothing ran. ⛔ **`skipped` IS NEVER `ok` WITH ZEROES.** "We could not look" and "we looked and saw nothing" are different facts, and collapsing them is precisely how twelve 401s became twelve `skipped` families behind an HTTP 200. The outcome enum is CHECK-constrained to ok|skipped|error and a non-ok outcome must carry its reason. NOT cron_runs: its counters carry metrics-row semantics written once per (mode, platform) by the cron shell and it has no jsonb column; overloading `rows_written` would make the existing monitor lie. Different grain, own table. The mechanism: `if (fillDays.length === 0) continue` fires AFTER the attempted increment and BEFORE the credential call on all five platforms (catchup:388/464/554/783/851), and `computeFillDays` returns [] for a connection with no rows at all (catchup:169-171, "NOT catchup's job") — so a connection that has never captured anything was STRUCTURALLY GUARANTEED to be counted as a success. **THE SECOND DEFECT IN THE SAME EXPRESSION:** errors were deduped by CLIENT while `attempted` counted CONNECTIONS, so one client failing on three connections yielded 3 attempted, 1 errored and TWO INVENTED SUCCESSES. Latent today only because no live client currently holds two connections on one platform (0 rows, measured) — a data accident, not a design property. ⛔ **THE FIX HAD TO MOVE INTO THE LOOPS, AND THAT IS FORCED, NOT PREFERRED. `SyncError` is `{ clientId, platform, message }` (catchup:82-86) and carries NO ACCOUNT IDENTITY IN EITHER LANE**, so counting errors per CONNECTION from the error list is not a harder query — it is missing data. The ledger is written where `conn.account_id` is in scope: `connLedger.begin()` beside each of the five attempt increments, `connLedger.mark(..., 'error')` beside all 26 error pushes, `connLedger.mark(..., 'ok')` at the five points where the base metrics_daily upsert actually succeeded. `attempted = ok + errored + skipped` BY CONSTRUCTION — the three states partition the attempted set, so they cannot drift apart the way two counters and a subtraction did. ⛔ **PRECEDENCE — error > ok > skipped.** A connection that wrote rows AND recorded an error is ERRORED: a PARTIAL FILL MUST NOT READ GREEN, and counting it once as an error is the conservative direction. A connection that wrote nothing and recorded nothing is SKIPPED, never a success. **SKIPPED IS A FIRST-CLASS OUTCOME** (migration 050, `cron_runs.connections_skipped`), which is [[LORAMER_EMPTY_CARRIES_ITS_DENOMINATOR_V1]] applied to a counter rather than to a pass: "we did not look" is a different fact from "we looked and there was nothing to do", and the old arithmetic had no room for a third state so it absorbed one into the other. An error recorded before identity resolves (the ga_tokens read that itself fails, catchup:838) is counted under an explicit `(unresolved)` account rather than dropped: still one attempted connection. ⛔ **THE SEATBELT, AND ITS RATIONALE IS THE WHOLE POINT: A MONITORING FIX MUST NOT BE ABLE TO CAUSE A MONITORING OUTAGE.** Every push auto-deploys but migrations are applied by hand, so the writing code can be live before the column exists — and PostgREST rejects the ENTIRE update on an unknown column, which would stop `finished_at` being stamped, and an unstamped cron_runs row IS the silent-hole signal. `finishCronRun` therefore retries WITHOUT `connections_skipped` and logs at ERROR volume that the skip count is being LOST, not defaulted to zero. Guarded by `tests/guards/connection-outcome-honesty.guard.mjs` (35th), red under six independent mutations, two of which mutate the transpiled ledger itself rather than the route text. | 2026-07-31 | do not relitigate.

- [LAW 2026-07-31 — GOVERNING] **LORAMER_ENTITY_STATE_HISTORY_V1 — NON-METRIC STATE GETS SCD TYPE 2, AND POLLING MAY NEVER ASSERT A CHANGE DATE IT DID NOT SEE.** ★NON-METRIC-STORAGE-SHAPE: 64 capture call sites all target `metrics_daily`, whose row is keyed by (client, platform, date, entity, breakdown). Configuration, entity-state SETS and change events have no shape that fits, so for two months they read as OUT OF SCOPE rather than MISSING — and an audit keyed to "does this land in metrics_daily" could never name them. `entity_state_history` (migration 048) is the missing shape: not summable, not keyed by day. **ADAPTED, NOT INVENTED (WEB-FIRST):** Fivetran History Mode appends a row per change and closes the prior one; Airbyte writes `_scd` tables where records are never deleted and effective date ranges denote validity; both track a DECLARED set only and both warn that frequently-changing tables inflate row counts. All three adopted. ⛔ **SCD2 IS THE BACKBONE, EVENTS ARE ENRICHMENT — and neither is sufficient alone.** Polling gives guaranteed coverage of every declared field at day granularity with no actor; change events give the exact timestamp, the actor and the field, but Google `change_event` has a ~30-day window and covers only some resources, so it can NEVER be the backbone. Events sharpen a transition polling already detected. ⛔ **`change_source` IS NOT OPTIONAL AND IS THE FIRST THING A REFACTOR WILL TRY TO DROP.** Polling yields the date we OBSERVED, never the date it CHANGED; if capture starts in November every first row reads valid_from = November, an artifact of us rather than a fact about the account. That is exactly Fivetran's caveat that `_fivetran_start` REPLACES the source timestamp. Three legal values, CHECK-constrained: `first_observation` (our first sighting — the start is UNKNOWN and the reader must say so), `poll_transition` (we saw A then B), `event`. The resolver exposes it as `startIsProven`. Without this column the table confidently asserts change dates it invented — ESSENCE law 6 in a new place. ⛔ **THE TRANSITION INVARIANT: AN UNCHANGED VALUE WRITES NOTHING.** A writer that re-observes daily and appends regardless puts 10k negative keywords × 365 = 3.65M rows/year in for ONE client — the inflation Fivetran names. Unchanged emits a `touch` that refreshes `last_seen_at` only, which is also what separates "unchanged" from "we stopped observing". The planner is PURE so this is provable without a DB. **`is_set` MAKES THE SCALAR INVARIANT DB-ENFORCEABLE — correcting the design report, which said it could not be.** Two partial unique indexes: one open row per (entity, state_key) WHERE is_set=false; one open row per member WHERE is_set=true. Enforced beats disciplined. Close-before-open is therefore mandatory ordering, not style. **AN ABSENT ROW IS UNKNOWN, NEVER FALSE** — `resolveEntityStateAsOf` returns a verdict with a reason (never_observed / before_first_observation / not_in_declared_set), never a bare value. "No row saying this term was negated" means we were not looking. **DECLARED SET, slice 1 only:** `advertising_channel_type`, `campaign_status`, `include_in_conversions` — all three ALREADY selected on existing GAQL (google-intelligence.ts:198/788, :386) and previously read once for the prompt and DISCARDED, so this adds ZERO API operations. Negative keywords are deliberately excluded: they are the only declared family with a row-count hazard and they want this invariant proven first. Guarded by `tests/guards/entity-state-scd2.guard.mjs` (33rd), red under four independent mutations (append-unchanged at 500-member scale, mislabelled first observation, open-without-close, absence-as-known-false). | 2026-07-31 slice 1 | do not relitigate.

The rule is that a cap you can MEASURE is a trade-off, while a cap you cannot measure is data loss wearing a round number. **THE INSTANCE:** `google-dimensional.ts` applied `LIMIT 300` (search_term) / `LIMIT 200` (keyword) INSIDE the GAQL with `ORDER BY metrics.cost_micros DESC`, so the tail was never FETCHED — not stored-then-trimmed, NEVER RETRIEVED — and Google search-term retention is ~90 days (see the ~90d line above), so each night's discarded tail became PERMANENTLY unrecoverable three months later. "300 every day" and "300 is all there was" are indistinguishable without the count; that is precisely how it hid. ⛔ **THE CAPS WERE NEVER A DECISION.** Nothing in DECISIONS, the QUEUE, ROADMAP or the two introducing commits (7919a16, d9bd0a6) justifies 300/200. SHIPPED forward-only: the two `fetchGoogleDimensional` queries are uncapped and the writer now reports `searchTermsFetched`/`keywordsFetched` (raw GAQL row counts, pre-aggregation). The WINDOW path (backfill/recover) still clips per day, deliberately and unchanged, but now records its pre-clip totals. Guarded by `tests/guards/capture-limit-is-measured.guard.mjs` (32nd) — red at 217d910 with 6 findings and red under a mutation that re-adds a generous `LIMIT 5000`. ⚠ HONEST LIMIT: the guard reasons over SOURCE TEXT (GAQL lives in template literals and it must run hermetically in `npm run build`), so it proves the LIMIT is absent and the counts are recorded — it cannot prove Google returned everything. | 2026-07-30 search-term cap flight | do not relitigate.

- [LAW 2026-07-30 — METHOD] **ACTIVE DAYS ARE THE DENOMINATOR, NEVER ALL CAPTURED DAYS.** A breakdown cannot exist on a $0-spend day, a no-order day, or a zero-session day — there is nothing to partition. Per platform: **Meta = account-grain `spend>0` · Shopify = account-grain `revenue>0` · GA4 = account-grain `extra->>'sessions'>0`** (GA4 has no spend, and sessions is the only delivery signal at the account grain). Counting inactive days MANUFACTURES false gaps by the thousand — the precedent is the 2026-07-19 Shopify geo false alarm, where geo "lagged" the account row on every store purely because geo rows only exist on days with orders. This is also what makes three of the nine Meta clients classify correctly rather than alarmingly: Foam OH went dormant 2025-09-30 and Influential Drones 2025-08-27, so their dead tails are excluded by construction instead of being counted and then excused; Thought Streams has 52 account days at $0 and zero active days ever, so its entire 25-family absence is TRUE ZERO rather than the largest hole in the fleet. | 2026-07-30 fleet measurement | do not relitigate.

SAME CLASS AS THE 2026-07-15 SEALED META BREADTH CURSORS reading 13/13 over a permanent hole: a completion flag is a claim the code makes about itself, and nothing was checking the claim against the data. **THE MISSING CHECK IS ONE COMPARISON — cursor `backfill_earliest_date` vs the family's actual `min(date)`** — and it is queued as ★COMPLETE-FLAG-AUDIT. - [DECIDED 2026-07-30 — WRITER FIX] **LORAMER_GA_DIM_COMPLETION_HONESTY_V1 — COMPLETION IS A CLAIM ABOUT WHAT WAS WRITTEN, NEVER ABOUT HOW FAR THE WALK GOT.** THE MECHANISM, recorded because it is not obvious and will be reintroduced by anyone who does not know it: `ga-dimensional-backfill.ts` decided `done = reachedStart || (earliestWritten <= targetStart && !errors && !timedOut)` — i.e. **WALK POSITION** — and the cursor advance `if (from < earliestWritten) earliestWritten = from` was **UNCONDITIONAL**, so it moved on months that wrote rows, months that were empty, AND months where families were skipped (the old comment said so out loud: "deepest month COVERED (empty or not)"). ⛔ **AND THE THING THAT MADE IT FIRE IS A RESILIENCE FEATURE: the per-family try/catch that stops ONE family GA cannot serve (age/gender without Google Signals, items on a non-ecommerce property) from breaking the other eleven.** The property-data-start detector counts CONSECUTIVE CLEAN-EMPTY months and `consecutiveEmpty` only increments when `skipped.length === 0` — so **ONE skipped family anywhere disables the floor detector**, the walk grinds every remaining month to HARD_FLOOR 2015-08-14 writing nothing, and the completion test is satisfied by distance travelled. Resilience and completion were in direct conflict and completion won wrongly; **completion now DEFERS to resilience by refusing to claim done** (`skippedCount === 0` added; the guarded advance only moves over a month that wrote rows or came back clean-empty). THE DEDUCTION, from code plus observed end state: all three affected clients (Foam OH · Influential Drones 5bb9b2ff · My Vacation Network) sit at the CONSTANT 2015-08-14, so `reachedStart` was FALSE (a true detection parks the cursor at a detected data-start, as it does for the other eight clients) — therefore the atFloor branch was the only path, and **a family MUST have been skipped, because otherwise `consecutiveEmpty` would have reached 6 across 100+ barren months and fired.** So the fix would have prevented all three. ⚠ **WHICH family threw is UNVERIFIED and needs a probe** — `cron_runs` stores only mode/platform aggregates (no drain step, no client_id, no per-family counts) and the 2026-07-15 laps are past the 7-day Vercel log retention. Why GA stopped serving at 2026-01-01 / 2024-02-01 / 2023-01-01 is likewise unverified. ⚠ **THIS FIXES THE ga_dimensional SETTER ONLY AND DOES NOT CLOSE THE CLASS** — `run-backfill.ts:~268` carries the identical shape for google/meta/shopify/woocommerce and the GA ACCOUNT cursor and is untouched (QUEUE ★COMPLETE-FLAG-AUDIT). SECOND occurrence of the class; the 2026-07-15 sealed Meta breadth cursors reading 13/13 over a permanent hole were the first, which is why this shipped with a guard rather than a note. | src/lib/backfill/ga-dimensional-backfill.ts, tests/guards/ga-dim-completion-honesty.guard.mjs | do not relitigate.
- [FINDING 2026-07-30 — TEST DESIGN, and it is the reusable half] **A BOOLEAN YOU CANNOT DRIVE IS A BOOLEAN NOBODY TESTS.** The ga_dimensional completion decision was INLINED inside a DB-driven, token-driven, time-budgeted walk, so nothing could exercise it without a live lap — which is exactly why it lied for two weeks with no test able to notice. **Extracting it into a pure exported function (`decideGaDimCompletion`) is what made the guard possible at all**, and it is the same move already banked twice: `shouldStartAnotherLap` (lap-budget.ts) and `dedupeBreakdownRows` (meta-simple-breakdown-core.ts). THE PATTERN, stated generally: when a decision inside an I/O-bound loop turns out to be wrong, extract the decision BEFORE fixing it — the extraction is not tidying, it is the only thing that makes the fix provable. ⚠ ALSO RECORDED, because an over-strict fix is its own defect: the guard asserts **reached-floor-CLEAN → complete ON PURPOSE**. A fix that made completion unreachable would leave every GA backfill walking forever and never marking done, which is the failure mode a careless guard would happily ship green. A guard for a too-permissive boolean must also pin the permissive direction. | tests/guards/ga-dim-completion-honesty.guard.mjs | do not relitigate.

Same law as the previous commit — completion is a claim about what was WRITTEN — violated by a different branch, which that commit did not touch. **WHY RESTART IS CORRECT AND NOT MERELY SAFE, and it is a structural argument rather than a preference: this branch is ONLY REACHABLE WHEN `backfill_complete` IS FALSE**, because a genuinely finished walk returns earlier at the `state?.backfill_complete` guard. So "cursor at/below the floor AND not complete" is **anomalous BY CONSTRUCTION** — either a walk that ended without covering its ground, or a human clearing the flag to force a re-walk. **Both want the same thing: walk again.** Re-walking ground that already has rows is WASTEFUL, NOT WRONG — every write is an idempotent upsert on the 7-col conflict key. ⛔ **"RETURN WITHOUT TOUCHING THE CURSOR" WAS REJECTED, and the reason is worth keeping: it stops the lie but leaves the cursor INERT FOREVER — re-entering 4x/day, doing nothing, recovering nothing. In the anomalous case that starts it AT 2015-08-14 — already at the floor — so after a single lap `decideGaDimCompletion` sees `earliestWritten <= targetStart` and **RE-SEALS the cursor**. The window restart alone would have read correct, passed review, and done absolutely nothing. It now DERIVES from the window: `earliestWritten = addDays(windowEnd, 1)`. **BYTE-IDENTICAL for a normal resume** (windowEnd = earliest-1, so +1 = earliest) **and for a never-walked cursor** (endDate+1); it differs ONLY in the anomalous case. It now flushes PER FAMILY and reports where to resume.** THE SHAPE, quoted from the pre-fix code because the brief described it as a per-month problem and it was worse than that: `recoverGaDimensionalForward` made ONE `fetchGaDimensionalRows({… startDate: from, endDate: to })` call for the ENTIRE requested range followed by ONE `.upsert(normalizeMetricsRows(rows))`. **There was no month loop at all** — `monthChunks` is used only by `runGaDimensionalBackfill` — so the atomic-nothing unit was the WHOLE RANGE, not a month. Verified live 2026-07-30: zero 2023-07 rows after the hang. Nothing corrupted, nothing written, the work simply lost every time. ⛔ **AND THE BUDGET NAMED IN THE BRIEF GOVERNED A DIFFERENT FUNCTION.** `DEFAULT_TIME_BUDGET_MS = 300_000` against `maxDuration = 300` is real and is checked only between months — but it is read at `runGaDimensionalBackfill` only. **The recover path had NO budget check anywhere**, so the fix was ADDING chunking and a budget where neither existed, not lowering a constant. THE FIX: `daySlices(from,to,10)` walked oldest→newest (a CALENDAR MONTH is measurably not survivable here — Foam OH months ran 6s to 229s and 2023-07 exceeded the 300s ceiling, so month-slicing would still die on the month that blocked the recovery); rows flushed per family through `upsertMetricsChunked` as each family completes; the budget consulted BETWEEN slices via the banked `shouldStartAnotherLap` AND INSIDE the slice before each family, because the 12 GA reports are where the time goes and a between-windows check alone overruns on the first window. Budgets moved to 240_000 (DEFAULT_TIME_BUDGET_MS 300_000 → 240_000, new RECOVER_BUDGET_MS) — a budget EQUAL to the ceiling passes its own check at t=299s and then overruns, which is LORAMER_META_ASSET_BUDGET_HEADROOM_V1 exactly. **ADAPTED, NOT AUTHORED:** `shouldStartAnotherLap` (lap-budget.ts) and `upsertMetricsChunked` both already existed with their own guards; re-implementing either would have been a second copy of a fact with a single owner. THREE PURE EXPORTS so the booleans are drivable — `decideGaRecoverCompletion`, `daySlices`, alongside the existing two; the third time in this file that extraction was the precondition for testing a boolean. ⚠ **notAttempted IS A SEPARATE BUCKET FROM skipped, and the distinction is load-bearing:** GA REFUSING a family is UNKNOWN coverage forever; running out of budget is coverage merely pending. Folding them would be LORAMER_DEGRADED_IS_NOT_FAILED_V1 again — a counter that cannot tell partial from total. `fetchGaDimensionalRows` gained two OPTIONAL hooks (onFamilyRows, shouldStop); a caller passing neither is BYTE-IDENTICAL, which is what keeps forward, catchup and the drain untouched. Per-family `mergeConflictKeyDupes` is EXACTLY equivalent to merging the assembled set because `breakdown_type` is IN the conflict key, so two families can never collide. The ceiling is RE-DERIVED from the route files rather than restated, so moving a maxDuration cannot leave the guard asserting a dead number. | LORAMER_GA_RECOVER_SUBMONTH_WINDOW_V1, 2026-07-30 | do not relitigate.

Taken literally, a window GA honestly served nothing for could never be marked done and a human would re-run it forever. So `decideGaRecoverCompletion` splits it: COVERAGE decides `complete` (every slice attempted, no throw, no skipped family, not timed out), ROWS decide a separate LOUD `rowsCovered` — console.warn'd and surfaced with an `emptyMeans` string. If ANY family was skipped or ANY slice missed, zero rows is NOT complete. **This is the same split LORAMER_RANGELAP_COMPLETION_HONESTY_V1 settled, MINUS its infinite-loop hazard**, and the difference is why it is safe here: that route rides a cursor the drain re-enters 4x/day, so a rows-required rule would re-walk forever; this route writes NO cursor, is human-invoked with explicit dates, and never self-re-enters. - [DECIDED + VERIFIED 2026-07-31 — BACKEND-ISOLATED] **LORAMER_GOOGLE_OP_BUDGET_LANE_ACCOUNTING_V2 — THE BUDGET REPRODUCED THE PRIORITY INVERSION IT WAS BUILT TO PREVENT: IT BILLED EVERY LANE FOR EVERY OTHER LANE'S SPEND.** `readGoogleRequestsToday` SELECTED `mode` from `cron_runs` and never read it (google-op-budget.ts:101-126, v1), summing all google modes into ONE total that `decideBudget(lane, ...)` then compared against a SINGLE lane's allocation. MEASURED 2026-07-31: the DRAIN was charged **~44,120 estimated ops against its 10,500 allocation** — of which catchup had spent 42,311 (96%), forward 1,809 (4%), and **the drain ZERO** — and was declined **58 times between 08:55:30Z and 13:40:31Z** while Foam OH and Veterinary mastermind kept bleeding recoverable Google geo at a day per day (both `google_geo` cursors still 2026-04-09, last moved 2026-06-28). **THE LANE THAT SPENT NOTHING WAS THE LANE THAT GOT STOPPED.** The arithmetic was internally consistent and only the ATTRIBUTION was wrong, which is why three days of ★GOOGLE-QUOTA-PRIORITY-INVERSION work did not catch it. **THE FIX IS THE FILE'S OWN HEADER COMMENT, FINALLY IMPLEMENTED:** forward and drain bill `connections_attempted` from their own mode's rows; catchup bills `days_filled`, because it fans out per GAP-DAY — that alone drops catchup's 2026-07-31 units from **421 to 207**, the 421 having been the same ~18 connections re-counted across 24 runs. ⛔ **`Math.max(conns, days)` IS GONE AS A LANE UNIT SOURCE.** It was v1's hedge against under-counting and it is precisely what let the re-counted connection figure outrank the real gap-day figure; a hedge that silently picks the larger of two DIFFERENT units is not conservative, it is wrong in a direction nobody can audit. It survives only on the unattributed branch, which is not a lane. ⛔ **THE DRAIN NOW RECORDS ITS OWN SPEND, AND THE BANKED REASON FOR NOT DOING SO WAS HALF FALSE.** The queue recorded that the drain "SKIPPED startCronRuns/finishCronRun to avoid editing the shared lib + an enum migration". VERIFIED AGAINST THE LIVE SCHEMA 2026-07-31: **`cron_runs.mode` is plain `text` and the ONLY constraint on the table is `cron_runs_pkey`** — no CHECK, no enum, no migration to avoid. Only the shared-lib half was ever true, and it is one line (`CronMode` gains `'drain'`). A LANE THAT CAN REQUEST BUDGET BUT CANNOT RECORD SPEND MUST NOT EXIST. **PLACEMENT IS LOAD-BEARING AND DELIBERATE: `startCronRuns` fires AFTER the quota / op-budget / forward-reserve gates**, so a DECLINED drain leaves NO unfinished `cron_runs` row — an unstamped row IS the silent-hole signal (LORAMER_CRON_RUNS_SENTINEL_V1), and a monitoring fix must not manufacture the alarm it exists to make readable; the decline is already durable in `capture_pass_log` via `recordLaneDeclined`. All three remaining exits stamp `finished_at`, and the drain's outcome tally is `attempted = ok + errored + skipped` BY CONSTRUCTION with precedence error > ok, per LORAMER_CONNECTION_OUTCOME_LEDGER_V1 (a claim-race loser is SKIPPED, not a success). **THE FLEET-CAP BACKSTOP SURVIVES THE PER-LANE FIX AND THAT IS NOT BELT-AND-BRACES:** `decideBudget` now applies TWO checks, either of which blocks — (a) this lane's spend vs its allocation, (b) the FLEET total vs the 15,000 cap — because the per-lane numbers are now correct but the operations-per-request ratio is STILL unknown (★GAQL-OP-METER), so 67 remains a LOWER BOUND in the unit that actually binds. The decline names WHICH check fired (`blockedBy`) and carries its denominator: lane, lane units, fleet total, both allocations, and the per-lane split (LORAMER_EMPTY_CARRIES_ITS_DENOMINATOR_V1). ⛔ **CATCHUP IS CONSTRAINED ON PAPER AND NOT YET IN PRACTICE — CORRECTED IN THE SAME FLIGHT, BEFORE THIS ENTRY SHIPPED.** The Gate-A report initially stated that catchup "IS newly constrained"; that was WRONG and is recorded as wrong. The arithmetic does constrain it — its 2026-07-31 figure is **20,804 estimated ops against a 4,500 allocation, 4.6x** — but catchup was **never declined once today** (zero `google_op_budget_catchup` rows against the drain's 58) and, by v1's OWN arithmetic, **22 of its 24 runs should have blocked, every run from 08:29:08Z onward**. It did real Google work through all of them. The gate is REACHED (cron/catchup:594 applies the hold BEFORE computeFillDays and is what makes fillDays empty, so the `fillDays.length === 0` ordering hypothesis is FALSIFIED) — so the budget READ itself is returning a stale near-zero total. **cron/drain carries `dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` + `fetchCache = 'force-no-store'` (route.ts:33-34); cron/catchup carries NEITHER** — the same un-hardened-route class as LORAMER_ORDER_GRAIN_NOSTORE_READ_V1, and the same root cause as the `entity_state_history` PK collisions found the same morning. The fix corrects WHO IS BLAMED; it does not manufacture headroom that does not exist. A reader cannot act on those identically — one is a fact about the account, the other is a fact about us — and alerting on the pair would fire hardest exactly where the data is heaviest. **THE ANATOMY, reported before anything was changed:** there were exactly **TWO** UNKNOWN returns in `resolveBreakdownCoverage`, reached from **FOUR** sites in `getBreakdownCoverage`. **PATH A** (`baseActiveDays == null || breakdownDays == null`) was reached from `if (error || !data)`, `if (!row)` and the bare `catch` — so it conflated a genuine read failure with "the RPC succeeded and returned nothing", ⛔ **and it CHECKED `error` AND THREW THE MESSAGE AWAY**, discarding the one fact that identifies a failure. **PATH B** (`baseActiveDays.length === 0`) covered THREE distinct states at once — not connected, connected-but-never-captured, and connected-with-no-activity-in-this-window — and asserted for all three that there was "no base-grain activity in this window". **THAT SENTENCE IS FALSE FOR TWO OF THEM:** a connection that has never captured has no activity in ANY window, and one that is not connected has no window to have activity in. So the only reason the code could ever imply was the one it had not measured. **THE FIX IS A DISCRIMINATOR, NOT A FOURTH STATE, and the distinction is the design:** the VERDICT answers "can I make a completeness claim about this window" — UNKNOWN is the honest no — while `unknownReason` answers the different question the reader needs next, WHY not. A fourth verdict would make an unmeasurable window look like a distinct kind of ANSWER rather than a distinct kind of SILENCE, would force every future reader to handle four cases, and would abandon the deliberate borrowing of google-quota-store's `'blocked'|'not_blocked'|'unknown'`. Three verdicts stand and are source-pinned; `unknownReason` is present IFF the verdict is UNKNOWN (asserted both ways — a COMPLETE carrying a reason fails). The four: `not_connected` · `never_captured` · `no_activity_in_window` (**the only one that is a fact about the ACCOUNT rather than about us**) · `read_failed`. ⛔ **THE PURE RESOLVER STAYS PURE.** The connection denominator does NOT come from `metrics_daily` — a client that never captured has no rows to be absent from, so the store structurally cannot tell "never captured" from "captured nothing here". The declaring table is `clients(deleted_at IS NULL) JOIN platform_connections`, which is what cron/sync itself uses to decide who to capture; the CALLER measures it and passes it in, and a SOURCE PIN fails the build if `resolveBreakdownCoverage` ever reads a table (otherwise the booleans cannot be driven without a DB and go untested). It is measured ONLY when base activity is empty, so COMPLETE/PARTIAL cost nothing extra, and "ever captured" is a limit-1 existence probe on the account triple matching migration 035's partial index, never a count. ⚠ **ONE JUDGMENT CALL, STATED RATHER THAN SLIPPED IN: when the connection denominator is NOT SUPPLIED, the result is `read_failed`, not `no_activity_in_window`.** Reporting the latter would assert that the connection exists and has captured before — neither of which was measured — which is precisely the defect being closed. A fifth reason for "undetermined" was considered and rejected: the enum is fixed at four and "we could not measure" is what `read_failed` means; the detail names which input was missing. **ALSO CORRECTED IN THE SAME FILE:** the comment claiming the RPC is "NOT APPLIED YET (migrations/046, authored not run)" — 046 and 047 were applied to production 2026-07-30, so the file was misstating the system it documents, in the place the next reader trusts. Corrected and SOURCE-PINNED so it cannot drift back. VERIFIED: blast radius re-grepped and is ZERO — exactly two files reference the three symbols (the definition and its guard), no production caller. Guard SEEN RED against pristine 5b84007 (exit 1, **22 findings** — 14 on silent UNKNOWNs, 4 on the read-failure collapse and the discarded error text, 3 on unattributed states). Leg (d) — no UNKNOWN path may return COMPLETE — did NOT fire at HEAD because that rule already held, so it was **MUTATION-PROVEN** instead of claimed: forcing the `not_connected` path to COMPLETE went RED, as did collapsing `read_failed` into `no_activity_in_window`; restored byte-identical both times. Every pre-existing leg preserved (they only ever asserted `verdict`, so nothing loosened). tsc 0; isolation with every changed file in a pristine worktree gave 36/36 ALL GREEN and `next build` 0. ⚠ **NOTHING READS THIS INSTRUMENT YET** — that is ★WIRE-COVERAGE-INSTRUMENT, which was blocked on exactly this, because you do not wire an instrument whose UNKNOWN means four different things. The window comes from `queryBreakdown`'s OWN resolution, never re-derived — `resolveDateWindow` is the one date resolver (Lesson 19) and a second one here would be free to drift from the rows it describes. The note is attached as its own `coverageNote` field because `result.note` already carries six other meanings (truncation, unknown family, wrong platform, geo grain, empty family) and overwriting it would trade one honest message for another. ⛔ **THE NOTE INSTRUCTS, IT DOES NOT INFORM — because attaching the verdict alone would only make it AVAILABLE to her, and ESSENCE law 6 requires it to CHANGE WHAT SHE SAYS.** PARTIAL names the hole count, the base-active total, the family and the actual dates (first 12, remainder counted) and then tells her to state the window is partial and name the gap, and never to treat a value's absence from the list as proof it did not occur. ⛔ **FOUR UNKNOWN REASONS GET FOUR DIFFERENT SENTENCES**, because collapsing them here would undo LORAMER_COVERAGE_UNKNOWN_REASON_V1 at the last inch and the reader is Lora: `read_failed` says the failure is OURS and carries the underlying error text · `not_connected` says not connected · `never_captured` says capture has never run so an empty ranking says NOTHING about the account · `no_activity_in_window` says it is a fact about the ACCOUNT and is **the only one of the four that licenses her to say the account was inactive**. ⛔ **COMPLETE RETURNS null.** Silence is the correct signal; a caveat that fires when nothing is wrong is the noise that made the nightly stale_tail flag fire fleet-wide on healthy clients. Catchup burned 13,869 of the fleet's 15,075 raw requests between 11:19Z and 11:59Z, the developer-scope token was exhausted, and **the sentinel still read NOT BLOCKED twelve hours later, because the only lane that could arm it was the only lane that made no Google calls that day** (the op budget declined the drain 180 times before it reached the API). RULE-HOME LAW, applied exactly: a rule that lives in one route and is broken in three others needs a home every lane necessarily passes through. ⛔ **THE BRIEFED PREMISE WAS FALSE AND IS CORRECTED ON THE RECORD: `withGoogleRetry` IS NOT A UNIVERSAL FUNNEL.** The live path and the forward path bypass it entirely (live uses `withGaqlRetry` + `safeQuery`; forward calls the fetchers directly), and it did not classify quota errors AT ALL — a quota error fell through to its transient test and was RETRIED three more times against an exhausted token. There is no single existing wrapper; there are FOUR boundaries, and all four now call `noteGoogleQuotaError`: `withGaqlRetry` (live + forward), `gaqlWithRetry` (campaign/adgroup backfill), `withGoogleRetry` (account adapter + dimensional backfill), and `safeQuery` (the 20 live sub-queries — the swallow point). The write is an idempotent upsert, so two boundaries observing one error is harmless, and it is AWAITED rather than fire-and-forget because a lambda that freezes after the response turns a background write into no write at all. GUARD `tests/guards/quota-sentinel-armed.guard.mjs`: leg (a) read-implies-write-or-an-explicit-reason, leg (b) all four boundaries arm, leg (b2) the swallow point MARKS and the answer path builds from an observed refusal. Seen RED at pristine HEAD on 6 findings; leg (a) cannot fire at HEAD (both non-writers are legitimately allowlisted) so it was mutation-proved three ways. ⚠ **AND THE MUTATION FOUND A DEAD BRANCH IN THE GUARD ITSELF — recorded because it is the argument for mutating rather than eyeballing a leg.** Mutation a2 (an allowlist entry with an empty reason) went RED, but on the WRONG message: the check read `if (READ_ONLY[rel])`, and `''` is falsy, so it fell through to the unallowlisted branch and the empty-reason check below it was UNREACHABLE CODE claiming to verify something it could never reach. A leg that goes red for the wrong reason looks identical to a leg that works, in every output a human reads. Fixed to `rel in READ_ONLY` and re-proved on the correct finding. | LORAMER_QUOTA_ARM_AT_ERROR_BOUNDARY_V1, 2026-08-01 | do not relitigate.
- [DECIDED + VERIFIED 2026-08-01 — THE ANSWER PATH] **LORAMER_QUOTA_OUTAGE_IS_NOT_ABSENCE_V1 — LORA MUST NEVER REPORT AN OUTAGE AS AN ABSENCE. THE CAVEAT IS NOW BUILT FROM AN OBSERVED REFUSAL, NOT ONLY FROM THE SENTINEL.** MEASURED 2026-07-31, and this is the user-facing half of the defect: `/api/intelligence` attached its quota caveat only when the sentinel ALREADY read paused (`if (qp.paused) googleQuota = qp`). With the sentinel stale-clear and the token exhausted, every dashboard load fired ~20 GAQL queries that were refused, `safeQuery` caught each rejection and returned `[]`, and the prompt carried NO outage line — so Lora received empty Google data and reported an absence as fact. **ESSENCE law 6 (never a confident answer over an uncaptured window) reached through the one code path built to prevent it.** THE FIX IS TWO-PART AND THE SECOND IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE: (1) `google-intelligence` now consults the sentinel before firing; (2) the route builds the caveat from the FAILURE ITSELF — a `GoogleQuotaError` rejection, or a fulfilled result carrying a `quota:true` entry in `fetchErrors` (a partial refusal where the base query survived). `safeQuery` MARKS quota failures for exactly this reason: arming alone is insufficient, because it returns `[]` either way and the caller still could not separate a refusal from a true zero. ⛔ **DELIBERATE DEVIATION FROM THE BRIEF, STATED ON THE FACE OF IT: the live gate reads `qp.paused`, NOT `holdGoogleWork`.** `holdGoogleWork` is TRUE when the sentinel READ FAILED, which is right for capture lanes (a false hold costs one lap) and wrong for the answer path — `google-quota-store.ts` banks it as do-not-relitigate that "a Supabase blip must never make Lora announce a platform outage that is not happening" (FAIL-PARTIAL READ-PATH LAW / VERIFICATION LAW 1). Gating the dashboard on `holdGoogleWork` would break every Google client's view on a DB hiccup unrelated to Google. **The capture lanes lose nothing:** catchup:272 and drain:83 still gate on `holdGoogleWork` BEFORE calling the fetcher, so the new check is a backstop beneath their gate, not a replacement. AND THE HOLD **THROWS** RATHER THAN RETURNING EMPTY — this file's whole contract is that a call which resolves (even to `[]`) is a TRUE ZERO while a rejection is a FETCH FAILURE; returning an empty PlatformIntelligence would write a false zero into that contract and let forward capture stamp cursors over a day it never fetched. `GoogleQuotaError` was already a possible rejection from this function (the base campaign query throws it via `withGaqlRetry`), so callers gain no new error class. ⚠ **GATE-B, STATED AT THE LIMIT OF WHAT WAS ACTUALLY PROVEN: the GATE (part 1) IS LIVE-PROVEN; the OBSERVED-REFUSAL FALLBACK (part 2) IS WIRED AND GUARDED BUT NOT LIVE-PROVEN.** The gate was demonstrated 2026-08-01 01:31Z — the fetcher refused up front, ~20 GAQL queries were never issued, and Lora answered a Google question by SAYING the quota was exhausted platform-wide until 08:03Z and sourcing the numbers from the captured store (law 6 and FAIL-PARTIAL satisfied in one turn). The fallback was NOT exercised, because **its precondition closed itself at 00:00:23Z**: midnight UTC rolled the day, the op budget reset, the drain finally reached the API, hit quota and armed the sentinel through the pre-existing `drain:314` writer — so by test time the caveat came from the SENTINEL path (`state:'blocked'`, which only that path emits) and the fallback branch was never entered. ⛔ **AND IT WAS DELIBERATELY LEFT UNPROVEN RATHER THAN STAGED:** recreating the precondition means WRITING to production to clear an armed sentinel, which would un-pause every capture lane against a genuinely exhausted token — reopening the exact hole the flight closed, in order to photograph it. A test that manufactures its own precondition proves the manufacture. The wiring is held by `quota-sentinel-armed.guard.mjs` leg (b2), seen RED at pristine HEAD; that is a static proof and is not claimed as more. | LORAMER_QUOTA_OUTAGE_IS_NOT_ABSENCE_V1, 2026-08-01 | do not relitigate.

The obvious move — add usage to the response — was REJECTED: it changes a PRODUCTION API's shape for a TEST HARNESS's benefit, and the constraint was explicitly additive-at-the-harness-layer. Instead the harness timestamps each chat call and, after the run, reads `anthropic_spend_log` for the window and attributes each row to the question whose window brackets it. `anthropic_spend_log` and `logSpend` are UNCHANGED and the read is read-only. **THIS IS ALSO WHAT MAKES THE ABORTED-BUT-BILLED CASE A LOOKUP RATHER THAN A BOUND:** the harness aborts at 120s (unchanged), the server completes and bills, and the ledger row lands AFTER the abort — so a row inside an aborted question's extended window IS the proof we paid for a discarded answer. Run 1 had seven and could only bound them. ⚠ **THE ATTRIBUTION IS VALID ONLY FOR A SEQUENTIAL HARNESS, and the reconcile REFUSES rather than guessing** — it checks for overlapping question windows and returns an explicit failure if it finds any, because a concurrent run would make the mapping silently wrong rather than visibly broken. The guard cannot prove either table matches ANTHROPIC; that is a vendor fact and its home is MODEL_PRICING's dated verification note. | LORAMER_EVAL_SPEND_LEDGER_V1, 2026-08-01 | do not relitigate.

MEASURED 2026-08-02: Foam OH's `google_geo` lap ran ~853s (DERIVED from the 12:20:20Z fire start and the 12:34:33Z cursor write — there is no lap timer, so it is two stamps and a subtraction, not an instrument reading), `google_user_geo` was dispatched into the remainder and killed at `maxDuration` with no cursor, no log line and no `cron_runs` stamp. It sat 35 days while every gate read green. ⛔ **FINDING 1, AND IT IS WHY "PAIR" UNDERSTATES THIS: `break` EXITS THE WHOLE STEP LOOP, NOT ONE ITERATION.** A slow step starves EVERY step behind it for that connection. On google the tail behind `google_geo` is user_geo → hour → age → gender → dimensional; on meta a slow `meta_campaign` sits ahead of FOURTEEN steps. Structural exposure is 29 of 34 steps; the LIVE victim set is far smaller because `curDone.includes(step.key)` skips completed steps — read live 2026-08-02, all three affected google connections (Foam OH, Inside, Veterinary mastermind) have 8 of 10 steps done and exactly `google_geo` + `google_user_geo` incomplete. Both facts belong in the same sentence. ⛔ **FINDING 2: THE REGISTRY'S ORDERING DEPENDENCIES LIVED ONLY IN PROSE AND ARRAY POSITION** — `google_geo` is "after google_campaign for grouping", `google_hour` is "After google_campaign so the anchor exists". Load-bearing, and nothing machine-readable could consult them, so ANY reordering would silently run a step before its anchor and the failure would read as a data gap rather than an ordering bug. **THE FIX:** a declared `tier: number` on every DrainStep makes that dependency legible — same tier ⇒ order-independent, and a step may only be reordered against its own tier. Within a tier, LEAST-RECENTLY-SUCCEEDED LEADS via `sync_state.updated_at ASC NULLS FIRST`. ⛐ **NO NEW STORAGE AND NO MIGRATION:** that column already exists and already moves ONLY on a successful lap (`writeRangeCursor` is its sole writer on this path; `rangeLap` returns before it on any non-200), so it already means "when did this step last actually succeed". Self-correcting — the moment the starved step lands, its sibling leads next. **DAY ONE IS ALMOST A NO-OP:** every step is alone in its tier EXCEPT `google_geo` + `google_user_geo`, both stateless-range, WRITE-ONLY, no reconcile, separate cursors, separate vendor views — neither is the other's anchor. **ALSO:** the bare `Date.now() - started > BUDGET_MS` dispatch gate is replaced by `shouldStartAnotherLap` (lap-budget.ts) with a `DRAIN_FIRST_LAP_MS = 900_000` reservation — LORAMER_META_ASSET_BUDGET_HEADROOM_V1's rule ("a between-iteration budget check is only safe if ONE ITERATION CANNOT EXCEED THE REMAINING CEILING") applied to the step loop rather than re-derived. ⚠ 900s is DERIVED FROM THE ONE ~853s observation, not measured; LORAMER_META_PRODUCT_ID_ROUTE_V1 banks that a per-unit cost inferred from a sibling was wrong by ~47% in the unsafe direction, so re-derive it the first time a lap is instrumented. ⚠ **INVERSION RISK, STATED NOT DISCOVERED LATER:** a step that can NEVER succeed keeps the oldest stamp and keeps leading. The reservation bounds it — an unaffordable lap is not dispatched, so the fire ends early and the sibling leads next — but if BOTH are permanently unaffordable, ordering cannot help and the honest signal is that neither cursor moves ([[LORAMER_FROZEN_CURSOR_DETECTOR_V1]]). VERIFIED: guard `tests/guards/drain-fair-share-order.guard.mjs` (49th), three legs — structural (every step declares a tier, non-decreasing across array index, EXACTLY ONE shared tier and it is the geo pair), behavioural (the REAL compiled `orderStepsFairShare` over five cases incl. Foam OH's actual stamps), source pin (`shouldStartAnotherLap` is the dispatch gate; the bare comparison is not). **SEEN RED BY MUTATION** — reverting to array order produced exit 1 naming both ordering cases with expected-vs-got. Working tree guard 48 PASSED / 1 FAILED, the 1 unchanged and still the held Tier-1 widen's own preconditions. ISOLATION at HEAD with the held widen absent: 49/49 ALL GREEN, `next build` exit 0, tsc 0. ⛔ **WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, and it is the honest half: fair-share gives the starved step a TURN, not an affordable lap.** It does not touch the cold-cache I/O root cause ([[LORAMER_COMPUTE_BASELINE_2026_08_02_V1]]), does not fix [[★DRAIN-CRON-RUNS-ORPHANED]], and cannot tell you `google_user_geo` will COMPLETE — only that it is dispatched first with the full budget instead of never, and is no longer dispatched into a window too small to hold it. Whether it finishes is the first live fire's answer. **SLICE 2 — the durable pre-dispatch attempt record that would end the silence — is NOT built: `capture_pass_log.outcome` is CHECK-constrained to ok|skipped|error and writing a pre-dispatch row as 'skipped' would destroy the very distinction [[LORAMER_EMPTY_CARRIES_ITS_DENOMINATOR_V1]] exists to protect.
