- [DECIDED + VERIFIED 2026-08-02 — STREAMING EMIT PATH + -NEXT CHAT UI, SHIPS INERT] **LORAMER_CHAT_STATUS_SUBJECT_V1 — THE ANSWER ARRIVES WHOLE; THE WAIT GETS A STATUS LINE THAT NAMES THE REAL WORK.** ⛔ **THE PRODUCT DECISION, RUSS, AND IT IS NOT A REDESIGN OF ★CHAT-STREAMING — IT IS THE OTHER SOLUTION TO THE SAME PROBLEM.** That item was escalated to demo-critical because Opus 5 median ~19s plus verbosity is *"unacceptable silence in a live voice-directed room"*. **THE PROBLEM IS SILENCE, NOT SLOWNESS** — [[LORAMER_LATENCY_IS_DILIGENCE_V1]] already settled that ~10s is a FEATURE and forbids cutting tool calls or downgrading the model to shave it. Progressive text is one answer; a status line is the one chosen: **"Reading Foam OH · Google · Nov–Dec 2024"**, not "Thinking…", not a spinner. Final-turn text renders on completion, in one piece. **WHAT SHIPPED, four parts.** (1) PROTOCOL — SUBJECT, NOT ARGS: the emit at claude-tools.ts carried `{name, preamble}` and nothing else, while `tu.input` sat in scope at that exact line; it now carries a subject built by the pure `toolSubject()` — who / which platform / which window (+ breakdownType for query_breakdown), across all three tools. ⛔ **THE RAW ARGS ARE STILL NOT ON THE WIRE** — shipping model-authored payload to a browser is a widening nobody reviewed, and the guard fails on any raw key. (2) PROTOCOL — FINISH: there was NO finish event; the emit fired immediately before `executeToolUses` and nothing emitted after. A finish now fires in a **`finally`**, carrying the Anthropic `tool_use` id as the correlation key. ⛔ **`finally` IS LOAD-BEARING, NOT TIDY:** a missing finish is the one outcome the client cannot recover from — the line hangs on "Reading …" and the user again cannot tell a slow turn from a dead one, which is the defect the whole feature exists to end. (3) RESOLVER — the client is resolved to a HUMAN NAME **server-side**: the bound `clientName` is already on the request body so the single-client case costs **ZERO extra queries**, and agency scope lazily fetches the RBAC-scoped roster once. ⛔ **NO ID EVER REACHES THE BROWSER, AND AN UNRESOLVED CLIENT OMITS THE SEGMENT** — never a UUID, never the string "unknown" (a claim we cannot support). `toolSubject` rejects a UUID-shaped name even if a future resolver hands one back. (4) UI — one fixed-height line, no wrap, ellipsis, stable leading icon slot so the text cannot shift when the subject changes; a **gradient sweep** via animated `background-position` + `background-clip:text` (not a pulse, not a spinner); and the **LM mark**, ONE mark in TWO states — Lora's message avatar on every turn AND the working indicator, CSS/SVG only, no Lottie, no JS frame loop. ⛔ **prefers-reduced-motion falls back to a STATIC WORKING STATE, NOT TO IDLE** — killing the animation must not also kill the information. **VERIFIED:** guard `tests/guards/chat-status-line.guard.mjs` (50th), three legs — (a) the BLOCKING loop and the SHARED `executeToolUses` emit nothing, which is what proves a non-streaming turn is unchanged (and with the flag OFF that is every production turn); (b) start/finish pairing driven against the REAL compiled streaming loop with the query layer **rigged to throw**; (c) no UUID in any emitted payload. **SEEN RED BY MUTATION** — removing the `finally` produced exit 1 naming the unmatched start id; restored and re-verified. Working tree 49 PASSED / 1 FAILED, the 1 unchanged and still the held Tier-1 widen. ⛔ **THE FLAG STAYS OFF. `LORA_CHAT_STREAMING` was NOT set** — this ships inert exactly as the streaming code did. ⛐ **AND IT INVERTS AN ACCEPTANCE TEST:** [[★CHAT-STREAMING-FLAG-FLIP]]'s test said progressive text is the pass and one lump means the flag comes back off. Under this decision one lump is CORRECT. That entry is corrected in place — the probe still matters (buffered frames break the status line too), but the pass condition is now **the status line CHANGES during the wait**. ⚠ ACCEPTED, NOT FIXED: the two legacy `dashboard/page.tsx` call sites pass no `onEvent` and will show nothing; -next is the build target and legacy was not touched. ⚠ WHAT NO STATIC GUARD REACHES: whether the line is legible, whether the sweep animates, whether reduced-motion is honoured on a real device — CSS facts a Node guard cannot see, and Gate-B on Chrome iOS per [[LORAMER_GATE_B_TARGET_IS_CHROME_IOS_V1]]. | LORAMER_CHAT_STATUS_SUBJECT_V1, 2026-08-02 | do not relitigate.
- [LAW 2026-08-02 — GOVERNING, RUSS; AMENDS [[LORAMER_WEB_FIRST_DIAGNOSIS_V1]] — NOT A SECOND LAW] **LORAMER_THREE_SOURCE_PRECONDITION_V1 — ALL THREE SOURCES ARE SEARCHED BEFORE ANY DESIGN, SPEC, OR BUILD INSTRUCTION IS WRITTEN. NOT BEFORE THE PASTE THAT ANSWERS IT — BEFORE THE INSTRUCTION EXISTS.** THREE-SOURCE — PRIOR CHATS: searched all 33 local session transcripts for `THREE_SOURCE_PRECONDITION` / `three-source` / `three-field header` / `HISTORY · WEB · REPO` / `prior art` / `header guard`. FOUND: the HISTORY · WEB · REPO header is REAL and HEAVILY USED — 343 occurrences of `HISTORY ·`, 290 of `WEB-FIRST HEADER`, 72 of `THREE-FIELD` — but ONLY EVER INSIDE A PASTE ("HISTORY · WEB · REPO — MIGRATION 045 APPLIED…", "— SESSION WRAP…"), never inside a committed artifact. `THREE_SOURCE_PRECONDITION` appears NOWHERE before today; every `THREE-SOURCE` hit is the unrelated ★SALES-CHANNEL-THREE-SOURCE-CONFLICT. A guard on the header was NEVER PROPOSED — zero hits for `header guard` / `guard the header`. So: the law exists, the practice exists, the ENFORCER has never existed. · WEB: searched ADR/decision-record enforcement and commit-trailer linting. FOUND, and it changed the design rather than merely confirming it: **archgate/cli** lints architecture decisions in CI with per-ADR executable rules; the ADR-skill ecosystem enforces required SECTIONS (several demand ≥2–3 "alternatives considered" with trade-offs) via output schemas in CI; **commitlint** + **danger-plugin-conventional-commitlint** are the standard way to gate commit-message fields. The load-bearing find is a WARNING, not a tool: ADR-first practice reports that *"one rubber-stamp ADR that an agent later references as prior art propagates bad reasoning forward"* — which is exactly the residual failure of a presence check, and is why the LIMIT below is stated in the law rather than in a footnote. Sources: github.com/archgate/cli · johnclick.ai/blog/adr-first-development-architecture-decision-records · commitlint.js.org/guides/ci-setup · danger.systems/js/plugins/danger-plugin-conventional-commitlint. · REPO: grepped every `.md`/`.ts`/`.mjs` for the candidate laws Russ named. FOUND FOUR OVERLAPPING, AND THE FIRST IS NEARLY THIS LAW: **[[LORAMER_WEB_FIRST_DIAGNOSIS_V1]]** (ESSENCE:154, 2026-07-19, WIDENED 2026-07-23) already mandates the three-field HISTORY · WEB · REPO header on any build/fix/detector/schema-change/diagnosis paste, and already states this exact limit — *"the header makes skipping VISIBLE; it cannot make searching HAPPEN — that stays discipline."* Also **SEARCH-BEFORE-NEW** (ESSENCE:142 + DECISIONS:74 — docs + prior chats before claiming novelty), **TWO-SOURCE VERIFY** (DECISIONS:22, 2026-07-09 — repo AND past chats; one store is not "verified"), **CLAIM-CONFIDENCE HARD VERIFY GATE** (DECISIONS:41 — anything a build depends on is verified through Claude Code BEFORE building). Existing enforcement surface: 49 guards in `npm run guard`, none touching provenance. — /THREE-SOURCE ⛔ **THEREFORE THIS IS BANKED AS AN AMENDMENT, NOT A NEW LAW** — Russ's own instruction ("if a law already says this, EXTEND it rather than adding a duplicate, and say which"). WHAT IS GENUINELY NEW, three things: **(i) THE GATE MOVES EARLIER** — WEB-FIRST binds the paste that ANSWERS an instruction; this binds the AUTHORING of the instruction. By the time the executor's header is due, a spec written from memory has already spent the budget. **(ii) IT BINDS THE AUTHOR, NOT ONLY THE EXECUTOR** — WEB-FIRST's enforcer is a header on Claude Code's paste, and everything corrected on 2026-08-02 was upstream of that paste. **(iii) THE ANSWER BECOMES A COMMITTED ARTIFACT** — the header moves off the ephemeral paste into the DECISIONS entry, which is the only form a guard can see. THE REASON, plainly: the recurring failure is work designed FROM MEMORY OR PATTERN rather than from sources, and on 2026-08-02 all FOUR of the day's corrections traced to a source that was available and not consulted — `synced_at` labelled a last-written stamp when `grep -rn synced_at src/lib src/app/api/cron` returns NOTHING and the column defaults to `now()` on INSERT; "statement timeouts stopped on 2026-07-29" retracted after the same query re-run route-filtered returned 50 AGAIN, proving 50 was a DISPLAY CAP and timeouts ran to 2026-08-01T17:15:24Z; a guard-count surprise on a docs commit that `node scripts/wrap-docs.mjs` already owned; and "neither cursor advanced at 18:20Z", read at 18:41:23Z when `google_user_geo` wrote at **18:44:17Z** — 2m54s early, and the instruction it would have justified was to REVERT A WORKING FIX. ENFORCER (RULE-HOME LAW — a repeat-offense rule needs an enforcer, not another entry): `tests/guards/three-source-header.guard.mjs`, 50th guard in `npm run guard`. SCOPE, mechanical and stated so it cannot cry wolf: fires on a DECISIONS entry whose tag carries a CONSTRUCTION verb — DECIDED / DECISION / SHIPPED / LAW — dated on or after the 2026-08-02 floor. Does NOT fire on **pure-fix or doc-only commits** (they bank no entry — the trigger is the ENTRY, never the diff), on **MEASURED / VERIFIED / FINDING / PROVEN / FACT** (a reading of the world is not a decision about it), on **RETIRED / CLOSED / SUPERSEDED / CORRECTION** (the sources belonged to the decision being unwound), or on the ~300 entries predating the floor — retro-stamping those would manufacture, at scale and in one commit, exactly the provenance this law exists to make real. Three entries banked EARLIER on the floor day are grandfathered BY NAME, not by a silent date cliff, so the exemption is finite and auditable. ⛔ **THE LIMIT, IN THE LAW ITSELF AND IN THE GUARD'S OWN HEADER — A REPO GUARD CANNOT OBSERVE WHETHER A CHAT SEARCH OR A WEB SEARCH HAPPENED.** They occur before any code exists, in a transcript the guard cannot read and a browser it cannot see. Nothing here proves a search occurred, was competent, or informed the design; what is enforceable is the ARTIFACT. The residual failure is RUBBER-STAMPING and it is published prior art, not our own worry (see WEB leg). A presence check cannot reach it — a human reading the legs can, which is why each leg must name WHAT WAS SEARCHED rather than report a verdict. The guard prints its DENOMINATOR on every run (total entries, how many predate the floor, excluded by tag, grandfathered, undated) so a vacuous green can never be read as a real one. | LORAMER_THREE_SOURCE_PRECONDITION_V1, 2026-08-02 | do not relitigate.
- [DECIDED + VERIFIED 2026-08-02 — DEVICE-DRIVEN FIX, STREAMING EMIT PATH + -NEXT CHAT UI, FLAG ON IN PRODUCTION] **LORAMER_CHAT_STATUS_FIRST_V1 — THE STATUS LINE WAS LAST ON SCREEN INSTEAD OF FIRST, AND THE MARK WAS DEAD ON WEBKIT. THREE DEFECTS, THREE DIFFERENT CAUSES, NONE OF THEM CSS TASTE.** THREE-SOURCE — PRIOR CHATS: searched all 33 transcripts for `pathLength`, `background-clip`, `stroke-dashoffset`, `text-fill-color`, `prefers-reduced-motion`, `onFirstTurnStarted`, `firstTokenP`, `Promise.race`, `do NOT commit to a stream`. `prefers-reduced-motion` 75 hits and `onFirstTurnStarted` 51 — the commit-gate design was discussed at length when [[LORAMER_CHAT_STREAMING_V1]] shipped, and the footgun it protects is banked. **BUT `pathLength` and `text-fill-color` hits are ALL from 2026-08-02 — i.e. my own V1 work and its commit message. WEBKIT'S pathLength BEHAVIOUR WAS NEVER SEARCHED BEFORE THE MARK WAS AUTHORED.** That is the finding, and it is an indictment of the V1 flight, not of the archive. · WEB: searched WebKit SVG and background-clip behaviour. TWO PUBLISHED FACTS, EITHER OF WHICH ALONE KILLS THE MARK: **(1) WebKit does not implement `pathLength`** — "WebKit doesn't yet do anything with the attribute. It just parses it, doesn't affect rendering"; Firefox and Blink scale dash values by it, WebKit does not. **(2) Safari does not support NEGATIVE `stroke-dashoffset`**, the standard self-erasing-stroke trick. For the sweep: WebKit's documented pairing for `-webkit-background-clip: text` is **`-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent`**, which WINS over `color`, plus a known Safari flexbox interaction and the documented 400%-background-size animation pattern. Sources: developer.mozilla.org/docs/Web/SVG/Attribute/pathLength · lists.w3.org/Archives/Public/public-svg-issues (WebKit parses-and-ignores) · svgator.com/help/technical-issues/path-animation-issues-in-safari (negative offsets) · twobrainy.com/posts/adventures-with-background-clip-and-flex-in-safari · dev.to/atila/gradient-texts-ppe. · REPO: read every LmMark call site (2 — both inside the `loading` branch, NONE in `messages.map`), the whole streaming loop, the route's commit gate, `chat-stream-read.ts` (forwards ALL events, so `status` needed no reader change), and `.tokens` on the portaled root (declares `--ink`/`--accent`/`--muted`, so the vars DO resolve — [[LORAMER_PORTAL_SEVERS_CSS_VARS_V1]] REFUTED as a cause here, not assumed). Existing surface: 50 guards; `chat-status-line.guard.mjs` passed all three defects. — /THREE-SOURCE ⛔ **D1 — THE STATUS LINE TOOK OVER A MINUTE. RUSS'S READING WAS RIGHT AND INCOMPLETE, AND THE HALF HE DID NOT HAVE IS THE DOMINANT CAUSE.** CONFIRMED: `'status'` was DECLARED in the `StreamEmit` union at claude-tools.ts:441 and NEVER EMITTED — grep returns the type and the channel comment, nothing else. **BUT EMITTING IT WOULD HAVE CHANGED NOTHING ON ITS OWN.** The route does not return its `Response` until `Promise.race([firstTokenP, settled])` resolves — a deliberate guard, because once SSE headers are written the status code is fixed and a 401/404/503 can no longer be expressed. `firstTokenP` was resolved ONLY from `stream.on('text')`. **When the model's FIRST turn goes straight to `tool_use` with NO preamble text — which is exactly what a data question produces — no text ever arrives on turn 1, so the gate stayed SHUT through the entire first model turn AND the tool execution.** Every `tool` event enqueued into a stream whose Response had not been returned; the user saw dots until the model began writing the FINAL ANSWER, then the whole queue flushed at once. THE FIX: `emit` is wrapped so the FIRST FRAME OF ANY KIND releases the gate (enqueue first, then release, so the frame is already in the stream when the Response returns), and a `status` is emitted immediately before every model call. THE FIRST EMITTABLE MOMENT, stated because it was asked: the top of the loop, immediately before the first model call — the SSE controller only exists inside the route's streaming branch, which is reached AFTER auth, RBAC and prompt assembly. WHAT IT CAN HONESTLY SAY THERE: **"Thinking…"** and nothing more. Not "Reading Foam OH · Google · Nov–Dec 2024" — no tool has been chosen, nothing has been read, and naming a client, a platform and a window there would be a FALSE STATUS, the same class as a spinner implying progress it cannot measure. After a tool returns, the numbers ARE in hand, so turn 2+ says "Working through the numbers…". THE TRADE, STATED NOT BURIED: releasing the gate on the first frame means model-chain exhaustion degrades from a JSON 503 to an SSE `error` event. The USER-VISIBLE RESULT IS IDENTICAL — `readChatResponse` normalises both to `error: 'overloaded'` and the client renders the same sentence — so what is lost is the HTTP status for observability. Auth/RBAC/assembly failures are unaffected: they happen before the loop is entered. RESIDUAL, NAMED: that pre-loop window is still silent. ⛔ **D2 — THE SWEEP DID NOT ANIMATE.** `-webkit-background-clip` was ALREADY PRESENT, so Russ's first candidate is REFUTED from the code, and the portal/CSS-var candidate is REFUTED too — `.tokens` is applied on the portaled root and declares all three tokens, and every value carries a literal fallback anyway. - [DECIDED + VERIFIED 2026-08-03 — SHARED CHAT COMPONENT ONLY, BOTH SURFACES, FLAG ON IN PRODUCTION] **LORAMER_LORA_SWEEP_TRAVEL_RANGE_V1 — THE SWEEP MADE THE TEXT DISAPPEAR, AND IT WAS ARITHMETIC RATHER THAN A WEBKIT QUIRK. THE RESERVED SPACE WAS ON THE WRONG SIDE BECAUSE `min-height` CANNOT SAY WHICH SIDE.** THREE-SOURCE — PRIOR CHATS: searched all 33 transcripts. `background-position` 91 hits, `loramerSweep` 61, `background-size: 400` 14, `ease-in-out` 20, `alternate` 28 — the technique has been discussed repeatedly this week, and **not once did anyone compute the covered range**. The 400% value was adopted from a published example on 2026-08-02 and the travel was then set to 100% → −100% by eye. Prior discussion is not prior verification. · WEB: searched animated gradient text and background-clip. **THE CANONICAL FORM OF THIS TECHNIQUE ANIMATES `background-position` FROM 0% TO 100% WITH `ease-in-out`** — "the animation works by animating the background-position property from 0% to 100% in a span of seconds with an ease-in-out timing function", with a background-size of 400–500%. That is EXACTLY the range this fix adopts and EXACTLY the easing, and every published example stays inside it. ⚠ **Stated honestly: none of the sources explains WHY leaving the range breaks it — they simply never leave it.** The mechanism below is derived from the background-position spec, not quoted. Sources: web.dev/articles/speedy-css-tip-animated-gradient-text · theadminbar.com/how-to-create-animated-background-gradient-text-with-css · amitmerchant.com/animated-gradient-effect-in-css · sitepoint.com/css-animate-text-gradients-patterns · dev.to/ibelick animated text gradient. · REPO: read the rule and the keyframes together rather than separately — which is what exposed it, because the defect exists only in the INTERACTION of `background-size: 400%`, `background-repeat: no-repeat` and the keyframe range. Also found the comment that used to sit on those keyframes asserting "a full box-width of gradient still in reserve at each end", which is FALSE, and which is why reading the comment instead of the arithmetic would have missed it a second time. — /THREE-SOURCE ⛔ **DEFECT 3, THE ONE THAT MATTERED — "flat text → gradient appears → text vanishes → returns". THE MECHANISM, DERIVED:** for `background-position: P%` with `background-size: 400%` on a box of width W, the image is placed at `offset = (W − 4W) × P = −3W × P`, and with `background-repeat: no-repeat` the image covers the box ONLY while `offset ≤ 0 AND offset + 4W ≥ W`, i.e. **`0% ≤ P ≤ 100%`**. The keyframes ran **100% → −100%**. At P = −100% the offset is **+3W** — the image sits ENTIRELY TO THE RIGHT of the box, the box has NO background, `background-clip: text` clips nothing, and with `-webkit-text-fill-color: transparent` **the glyphs render FULLY TRANSPARENT.** Half of every cycle was spent walking the gradient off the text. That is the vanish, and it is the position spec, not a repaint bug. FIXED: travel is now **100% → 0%**, inside the covered range at every frame, so some legible colour is on every glyph at every moment and the line CANNOT disappear. Because the gradient's outer thirds are flat `--muted`, both endpoints render as uniform muted text — it parks at neither end looking broken, which is what "calm, not broken" requires. TIMING: **6s, `ease-in-out`, `alternate`** (was 2.4s linear). 6s per traversal — a 12s round trip — because at 2.4s the highlight crossed ~25 times a minute and read as a blink; `ease-in-out` because the highlight must slow where the reversal happens; and **`alternate` is what removes the SNAP** — a one-way animation must jump from its end value back to its start on every iteration, and that discontinuity is one no easing can hide. ⛔ **DEFECT 1 — THE RESERVE WAS ON THE WRONG SIDE, AND `min-height` IS THE REASON.** A min-height says how TALL a block is and says NOTHING about which side the slack falls on; the surrounding flex chain decided, and it decided BOTTOM — mark and line hard against the composer with the emptiness above. It is now an explicit `.reserve` spacer element rendered AFTER the status line: mark first, line second, emptiness third, and DOM order settles it where no ancestor can. `.turn` also now declares `justify-content: flex-start` rather than relying on the initial value. **DEFECT 2 — THE GAP.** The container's blanket `gap: 6px` applied between every child; it is now `gap: 0` with explicit per-child values measured against the artwork rather than eyeballed: `.mark { margin-top: -4px }` cancels the ~4.1px of empty box ABOVE the strokes so the mark hugs the message above it, and `.statusText { margin-top: -6px }` cancels most of the ~8.4px BELOW them, leaving **~2px** of optical space so the two read as one unit. (The artwork spans y 2.9→18.1 of a 24 viewBox at a 34px box; change the viewBox and both numbers change.) FIX-WITH-GUARD — `chat-status-visible.guard.mjs` gains legs (c2) and (c3). (c2) RE-DERIVES THE COVERED RANGE from the declared `background-size` and `background-repeat` and fails on any keyframe outside it, so the vanish cannot return; plus a 4s duration FLOOR and a not-`linear` easing assertion. (c3) fails on `background`/`border`/`border-radius`/`box-shadow` on `.turn`, on a returning `min-height`, and on any child order other than mark → status → reserve. SEEN RED FOUR WAYS by reverting each fix: *"THE SWEEP TRAVELS TO -100%, OUTSIDE [0%, 100%] … THE STATUS LINE DISAPPEARS"* · *"the sweep runs at 2.4s … reads as a BLINK"* + *"the sweep is 'linear'"* · *".turn declares 'background'"* + *"'border-radius'"* · *"children are not in the order mark -> status -> reserve (indices 181, 413, 137)"*. ⚠ **A LATENT CRASH WAS FOUND AND FIXED IN THE GUARD ITSELF:** the reduced-motion finding carried an unescaped backtick pair inside its template literal, so JS parsed it as arithmetic on undefined identifiers. `node --check` PASSED and the guard ran green because the string is only BUILT when the finding fires — **a guard whose FAILURE path throws reports nothing at the exact moment it matters.** ⛔ **WHAT IS STILL NOT ASSERTABLE FROM NODE, and it is most of what "calm" means:** whether the motion reads as continuous, whether ~2px is the right gap, whether 104px is the right reserve, whether the highlight is perceptible at all. Those are rendering judgements needing a device — Gate-B on Chrome iOS ([[LORAMER_GATE_B_TARGET_IS_CHROME_IOS_V1]]). Queues is in **PUBLIC BETA**; trigger is `functions.<path>.experimentalTriggers` type `queue/v2beta`; `@vercel/queue` is published at **0.4.0**. Confirmed every property this design leans on: **at-least-once delivery · idempotency keys · per-consumer-group max concurrency · per-deployment topic partitioning · 24h default retention (60s–7d max) · a queue trigger makes the route PRIVATE with no public URL.** Sources: vercel.com/docs/queues/concepts · /quickstart · /sdk · vercel.com/changelog/vercel-queues-now-in-public-beta · npmjs.com/package/@vercel/queue. · REPO: read `metrics-upsert.ts` for the 7-column conflict key (where idempotency actually lives), `google-op-budget.ts` for the allocate-before-spending posture, and confirmed **no prior Queues usage anywhere in the tree**. — /THREE-SOURCE ⛔ **AVAILABILITY, BOTH HALVES, BECAUSE ONLY ONE COULD BE PROVEN WITHOUT FIRING:** the PLATFORM half is confirmed (public beta, SDK published, docs explicit). The **ENABLED-ON-THIS-PROJECT** half could NOT be confirmed without publishing a message, which was forbidden — the Vercel MCP exposes no queues listing. **THE DEPLOY IS THE ENABLEMENT TEST** (an unrecognised `experimentalTriggers` entry surfaces at build/deploy), and its result is reported rather than assumed. No fallback to a cron loop was written: the durable retry is the entire point, and a silent fallback would have delivered the shape without the property. **1 · WORK UNIT** — one message = one client × one universe entry × one date window, carrying its FULL context because Queues gives **no FIFO guarantee** and nothing may be inferred from order. **IDEMPOTENCY IS CONFIRMED, NOT ASSUMED:** the writer goes through `upsertMetricsChunked` on the 7-column natural key, and the guard drives the SAME message TWICE against the real compiled writer and compares emitted payloads — identical keys, identical bytes. **2 · RETENTION — SELF-RE-PUBLISH, NEVER PRE-PUBLISH.** A walk to 2022-03-05 is ~50 months and would outlive any TTL, so each consumer captures ONE 30-day window and publishes the NEXT only if the vendor still returned rows, keyed `client|entry|window` so a redelivery cannot fan out a second walk. The queue holds **O(1) messages per entry instead of O(months)**. **3 · GOVERNOR** — reserves **4,000 ops for forward sync + 5,000 for the drain of 15,000**, leaving the backfill **6,000/day**, and returns ZERO once spent rather than discovering the cap; every decision carries an auditable denominator. ⛔ **THE OPS RATIO REMAINS UNMEASURED AND IS NOT PRETENDED OTHERWISE: the Google Ads API returns NO operation count on a search response — no response field, no header the client exposes; the only source is the API Center UI, a human read.** The governor counts REQUESTS and converts with `ASSUMED_OPS_PER_REQUEST = 10`, deliberately pessimistic and stated in the data. [[★GAQL-OP-METER]] stays open. **4 · COMPLETION** — `universe_run_state.vendor_exhausted_below` is a DATE written ONLY alongside the writer's `exhaustion_proof`. ⛔ **NOTHING WRITES `backfill_complete`** — that column reads TRUE on 214 cursors while Google still serves years more, and the guard fails on the string. DONE = every selectable entry settled (exhausted or recorded-skipped); **an entry with NO row is explicitly NOT settled, because never-asked ≠ asked-and-empty** — the exact conflation this arc exists to end. **5 · NOTICE** — `universe_run_notice`, a QUERYABLE row per completed client (entries total/exhausted/zero/skipped, rows written, requests spent, per-surface `detail` jsonb), not an email. **6 · ⛔ THE ENTITY-LEVEL AXIS IS NOT IN THIS FLIGHT AND I AM SAYING SO RATHER THAN SHIPPING ACCOUNT-ONLY AND CALLING THE UNIVERSE CAPTURED.** Flight 1 writes `entity_level='account'` only; this flight does not change it. Campaign / ad_group / ad is **FLIGHT 3**. FIX-WITH-GUARD: `universe-runner.guard.mjs`, 55th. **THREE LEGS, ALL SEEN RED IN THROWAWAY WORKTREES:** (a) key varied per delivery → *"THE SECOND DELIVERY PRODUCED DIFFERENT CONFLICT KEYS… a redelivered message must land on the SAME 7-column key or it duplicates rows"*; (b) reserves zeroed → *"the backfill allowance (15000) is not less than the daily cap (15000) — nothing is reserved and the forward sync can be starved"*; (c) `backfill_complete` + a clock + a cron added → *"references backfill_complete… a path built to end that defect may not inherit the column that carries it"* + *"vercel.json has 1 CRON entr(ies) pointing at the universe path… starting it is Russ's call in a separate turn"*. ⚠ **AND GATE-A CAUGHT A REAL DEFECT OF MINE BEFORE IT SHIPPED, WHICH IS WHY THE COUNT RULE EXISTS.** The working-tree FAILED count went 1 → 2: `token-freshness-and-validation` found *"2 NEW route(s) read a *_tokens table without force-dynamic + force-no-store"*. Both new routes reach `google_tokens`/`platform_connections` and had `force-dynamic` but not `force-no-store`. **A CACHED REFRESH TOKEN ON AN UNATTENDED 3AM QUEUE CONSUMER IS THE PERFECT SILENT FAILURE** — the fetch fails auth, the message retries, and nothing looks broken ([[LORAMER_NO_CACHED_DB_READ_V1]]). I STOPPED at the count change, reported, and fixed only on instruction; the count is back to exactly 1. MIGRATION 051 **APPLIED 2026-08-03** (verified live: `universe_run_state` 13 columns, `universe_run_notice` 11) — two ADDITIVE tables, no existing table touched, no conflict-key change. Applying the SCHEMA is not starting the RUN: the tables are empty and nothing publishes until the starter is fired. ⛔ **WIRED, NOT FIRED — VERIFIED: vercel.json carries 16 crons before and after, and ZERO cron paths matching "universe".** The consumer's queue trigger makes it private; with no messages published it never runs. **WHAT STARTS IT:** `POST /api/backfill/universe-start?clientId=<id>&endDate=YYYY-MM-DD` with `Authorization: Bearer $CRON_SECRET`, `&dryRun=1` to publish nothing. The documented behaviour is exact: `outputFileTracingIncludes` takes **route globs as keys and project-root-relative globs as values**, and exists precisely for "cases where automatic file tracing fails". From day 2 every entry touched today reports its LIFETIME spend**, so the governor bills the walk for yesterday, `remainingOps` goes to zero within the first few hundred messages, and a 3-day unattended walk halts on day 2 emitting *"backfill allowance EXHAUSTED for today"* — a true-sounding sentence about a lane that had spent almost nothing that day. ⛔ **SAME FAMILY AS [[★DRAIN-CRON-RUNS-ORPHANED]] AND AS THE 2026-08-04 `now()`-vs-`clock_timestamp()` STAMP: a column whose name promises one thing and whose value means another.** THE FIX IS A SHAPE CHANGE, NOT A BETTER SUM — `universe_window_log` holds ONE ROW PER WINDOW, so summing today's rows is today's spend by construction and there is no accumulation left to get wrong. The defective read is now guarded out of both routes by name (`universe-window-log.guard.mjs` leg (c)), because the fix is a one-import regression away. | LORAMER_UNIVERSE_GOVERNOR_SPEND_WAS_CUMULATIVE_V1, 2026-08-04 | do not relitigate.
- [MEASURED 2026-08-04 — N=7, AND IT IMMEDIATELY CHANGED A CONCLUSION] **LORAMER_STEADY_STATE_MEDIAN_AND_SPREAD_V1 — A SINGLE LATENCY READING CANNOT TELL DRIFT FROM NOISE, AND A TIER DECISION WAS ABOUT TO BE MADE ON ONE.** The cold geo read was reported at **2,624 ms**, then **4,320 ms**, then **4,835 ms** on three consecutive runs of the same script against the same data — an 84% spread. `scripts/steady-state-measure.mjs` now takes **N=7** readings and reports **median · min–max · spread as a % of median · IQR · every raw sample**, and flags any spread over 40% as *"noise, not a measurement — do NOT make a tier decision on this number."* ⛔ **THE LIMIT IS STATED ON THE FACE OF THE OUTPUT RATHER THAN ENGINEERED AROUND: COLD IS INHERENTLY n=1.** "Cold" means first-touch-after-restart and managed Postgres offers no way to evict the buffer cache on demand, so N cold readings would need N restarts. THAT LAST FIELD IS THE DISCRIMINATOR: a frame carrying "Reading Foam OH · Google · ..." logged beside a `renderedStatus` of "Working..." proves arrival and convicts the render, in one line. TIMESTAMPS ARE THE POINT, NOT THE FRAME NAMES — the three silent windows ([[★CHAT-STATUS-SILENT-WINDOWS]]) have never been measured, and inter-frame gaps are what turn "8 frames arrived" into "and there were 94 seconds of silence here". AND THE SEND HALF SETTLES AN OPEN QUESTION: every `send()` is stamped with `msSinceLastInput`, `hadRecentInteraction` and a length + cheap digest — NEVER THE TEXT — so a programmatic re-fire (no interaction behind it) is distinguishable from a keystroke, closing the "was the second send automatic or Russ retyping" gap that [[★CHAT-FIVE-DEFECTS-2026-08-05]] left explicitly unresolved. He reversed it on the measurement — an answer already arriving is a better signal than any line describing it. ⚠ **THE `answer` EVENT REMAINS AUTHORITATIVE:** the streamed text is a PREVIEW, never in `messages`, never persisted, discarded the instant the real one lands — so it can never become a second source of truth. And **one indicator per turn still holds**: the working mark and the streaming bubble are mutually exclusive arms of the same branch. **S2 — CONCURRENT SUBJECTS AGGREGATE INSTEAD OF OVERWRITING.** The probe proved the mechanism rather than suggesting it: tool frames land **0-2ms apart** (seq 11→12 was ONE millisecond), and `renderedStatus` showed the previous line at seq 11 and seq 11's own subject at seq 12 — displayed for about a millisecond before seq 12 replaced it. A `MIN_SUBJECT_MS = 1200` floor DEFERS a too-fast replacement rather than dropping it. **S3 — SPLIT, AND ONLY HALF IS FIXABLE.** ⛔ **THE 107-SECOND COMPOSING GAP CARRIES NO FRAME, NO TOKEN AND NO COUNT, SO NOTHING TRUTHFUL EXISTS TO SHOW, AND NOTHING WAS INVENTED.** ⚠ **AND THE 35-SECOND OPENING IS DIAGNOSED BUT DELIBERATELY NOT FIXED, because the fix trades against a banked rule:** the status frame IS enqueued at the RBAC gate, but `return new Response(stream)` sits at `route.ts:336` — **AFTER both awaited `/api/intelligence` fetches at :171 and :208** — so the browser receives no headers, and therefore no frame, until they finish. - [SHIPPED 2026-08-06 — LIVE-PATH, PRODUCTION, RUSS APPROVED; FLIGHT 2 OF 2, LANDED IN THE ONE WINDOW WHERE IT IS INERT] **LORAMER_GOOGLE_OP_BUDGET_BACKFILL_LANE_COUNTED_V3 — THE FLEET CEILING COULD NOT SEE ITS LARGEST SPENDER, AND THE ZERO IT READ WAS NOT A MEASUREMENT.** **THREE-SOURCE — PRIOR CHATS: no chat-search tool; searched the repo's own transcript of the arc instead — the 2026-08-05 opener in CONTINUE_HERE/§E, DECISIONS LORAMER_FLEET_CEILING_HAS_A_PRIORITY_ORDER_V1, and QUEUE ★GOOGLE-QUOTA-PRIORITY-INVERSION (2). FOUND: this flight was SPECIFIED IN ADVANCE, down to the blocker order — "source the backfill lane's spend from `universe_window_log` and count it in the fleet total. IT MUST NOT SHIP UNTIL THE WALK'S OWN OVERSPEND HAS AGED OUT". Nothing here is a new idea; the only new thing is that BLOCKER A has now passed. · WEB: searched Google's own quota accounting and the general problem of one counter fed by heterogeneous sources. FOUND, and it CONFIRMED the shape rather than changing it: Google enforces the 15,000/day limit AT THE DEVELOPER-TOKEN LEVEL so every account and every job type shares one bucket — which is exactly why a lane invisible to the counter is not a cosmetic gap; and the published multi-tenant quota pattern is a MULTI-DIMENSION COUNTER that "can charge different units from different sources", one request consuming multiple units, which is precisely this change (cron_runs lanes charge work-units × 67, the walk charges requests 1:1, both into one fleet total). Also noted and NOT acted on: requests failing without a GoogleAdsFailure are not billed, so our count stays a ceiling. Sources: developers.google.com/google-ads/api/docs/best-practices/quotas · developers.google.com/google-ads/api/docs/api-policy/access-levels · metacto.com/blogs/llm-rate-limiting-token-quotas-production. · REPO: read `src/lib/backfill/google-op-budget.ts` in full (357 lines), `universe-window-log.ts`, `migrations/054_universe_window_log.sql`, `migrations/057_universe_lane_spend_fn.sql` and the existing `tests/guards/google-op-budget.guard.mjs` in full BEFORE editing. What shipped was `backfill: units.backfill * GAQL_REQUESTS_PER_CONNECTION_DAY` where `units.backfill` is never incremented, because the universe walk writes NO `cron_runs` row. **That is not a measurement that happens to read zero; it is arithmetic that CANNOT read anything else** — a permanent zero wearing the costume of data, in the one field the ceiling most needed to be honest about. MEASURED CONSEQUENCE, 2026-08-05: walk 13,230 raw requests invisible + `cron_runs` 2,412 visible = **15,642 against a 15,000 cap, while every lane's own check read healthy.** The fleet backstop was measuring ~15% of the fleet. ⛔ **THE FIX.** The lane is now sourced from `universe_window_log` — the walk's own durable per-window ledger — through the SAME `universe_lane_spend_today` aggregate (migrations/057) the walk's own governor reads. **ONE READER, NOT TWO:** `readLaneSpendToday` is IMPORTED rather than re-implemented, because both files already carry the warning that their day boundaries must move together and a second RPC call site here is exactly the drift they warn about. It gained an OPTIONAL `since` parameter (additive; the walk still calls it with no argument and its behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged), and `readGoogleSpendToday` passes its OWN `since` in, so both halves of the fleet total are provably the same day rather than two independently-computed midnights that agree by luck. ⛔ **THE UNIT TRAP, AND IT IS THE ONE THING THIS CHANGE COULD HAVE GOT CATASTROPHICALLY WRONG: `universe_window_log.requests_spent` IS ALREADY IN REQUESTS.** The other three lanes store WORK UNITS (connections, gap-days) and multiply by `GAQL_REQUESTS_PER_CONNECTION_DAY` = 67. Multiplying the walk's figure too would over-state it 67× and refuse every lane on a ceiling that does not exist — a self-inflicted fleet outage dressed as a safety fix. The `units` map no longer carries a `backfill` key AT ALL, so no future reader can pick it up by accident. ⛔ **FAIL-CLOSED IS PRESERVED AND IT IS THE DESIGN, NOT AN OVERSIGHT:** `readLaneSpendToday` THROWS on an unreadable ledger, the throw is caught by `readGoogleSpendToday`'s own catch, which returns null → `decideBudget` yields `'unknown'` → EVERY LANE HOLDS. Defaulting the walk to 0 when its ledger is unreadable is the precise shape of the 2026-08-05 defect that authorised ~10,800 consecutive publishes — an unreadable counter arriving as "nothing spent" is the most permissive answer a governor can be handed. ⛔ **THE TIMING IS LOAD-BEARING AND IS THE REASON THIS COULD NOT SHIP YESTERDAY.** Landing this on 2026-08-05 would have counted 13,230 trailing walk requests against the 15,000 cap and blocked forward, catchup and drain the moment it deployed — the exact outage the budget exists to prevent, caused by the fix for it. It landed with the walk HALTED and its trailing-24h spend READ LIVE AT 0 (`universe_window_log`: 0 windows since 2026-08-05 14:19:18Z), which is the only window in which the change is inert on arrival. **FIX-WITH-GUARD, SAME COMMIT, AND BOTH HALVES WERE SEEN RED.** The existing `google-op-budget.guard.mjs` was EXTENDED rather than duplicated (no new guard file; the denominator is unchanged at 63). **STATIC leg (j), in `npm run guard` so Vercel enforces it on every build:** asserts the lane is sourced from `readLaneSpendToday`/universe-window-log, that no `units.backfill` key exists, that the value is NOT multiplied by `GAQL_REQUESTS_PER_CONNECTION_DAY`, that the read sits INSIDE the try whose catch holds every lane, and that it reuses the cron_runs `since`. **SEEN RED against pre-fix HEAD — 7 findings, each naming the real defect** (run against a THROWAWAY COPY via `LORAMER_GUARD_ROOT`, never by mutating the real files — [[★GUARD-BREAK-AND-RESTORE-IS-UNSAFE]]; post-fix → backfill lane reports **exactly 12542**, exit 0. ⚠ **AND THE LIVE LEG DECLARES ITS OWN DENOMINATOR RATHER THAN PRINTING A BARE PASS:** with the walk halted the trailing 24h is empty, so on today's run it prints *"VACUOUS today — it asserts nothing about the counting; the static legs (j) do."* A green that means "there was nothing to check" must never read like a green that means "the counting works" ([[LORAMER_EMPTY_CARRIES_ITS_DENOMINATOR_V1]]). ⚠ **WHAT THIS DOES NOT FIX, NAMED SO A GREEN RUN IS NOT OVER-READ: THE DAY MODEL IS STILL WRONG UNDERNEATH BOTH HALVES.** Every reader counts from 00:00Z while Google bills a SLIDING 24-hour period; counting since midnight UNDER-counts against a sliding window, which is the direction that walks into RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED believing there is headroom. - [SHIPPED 2026-08-06 — -NEXT UI ONLY (BOTH CONTAINERS), FLIGHT A OF 2, RUSS APPROVED BOTH HALVES] **LORAMER_CHAT_COPY_BLOCKS_V1 — THE BUTTON. AND THE QUEUE ENTRY SAID, IN ADVANCE, THAT THE BUTTON ALONE IS THE FAILURE MODE.** **THREE-SOURCE — PRIOR CHATS: no chat-search tool; searched the repo's own record instead — QUEUE ★CHAT-COPY-BLOCKS, DECISIONS LORAMER_CHAT_SHARED_THREAD_V1, LORAMER_LORA_WORKING_SHARED_V1 and LORAMER_LORA_PAGE_ICONS_V1. FOUND, AND IT CHANGED THE FLIGHT: the entry states "it is TWO parts, not one… ⛔ Build the button alone and it will look shipped and be useless on the exact answers it exists for." The brief as first written scoped only the button; I stopped and said so BEFORE writing code, and Russ approved both halves. Flight B is the prompt. · WEB: NOT SEARCHED, and stated rather than implied — the mechanism here is react-markdown's own documented `components` prop, read from the installed version's API, and the copy is `navigator.clipboard.writeText`. No published pattern was adapted and none was needed; the two real constraints (no icon webfont outside Shell, no state across a client switch) are this repo's own banked defects, not industry ones. ⚠ Per LORAMER_WEB_FIRST_APPLIES_TO_LAYOUT_V1 the positioning question WOULD have warranted a search had I invented a layout; I did not — `position: relative` + an absolutely-positioned child is the shape the stylesheet already uses elsewhere. · REPO: read `LoraThread.tsx` in full (249 lines) BEFORE editing, plus `lora-thread.module.css`'s `.md` block, `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx`'s six ReactMarkdown call sites, `chat-status-truthful.guard.mjs` and `scripts/run-guards.mjs`. Established rather than assumed: LoraThread is the ONE renderer for BOTH -next containers, legacy carries its own six call sites and imports neither the component nor its stylesheet, and react-markdown ^10.1.0 + remark-gfm ^4.0.1 are already present so NO new dependency was added. — /THREE-SOURCE ⛔ **WHAT SHIPPED.** A `components={{ pre: CopyablePre }}` override on the -next markdown path: a copy button on every fenced block, inline-SVG icon, copied-state tick, `aria-label` that tracks the state. **IT WORKS MID-STREAM BY CONSTRUCTION, NOT BY A COMPLETION CHECK** — react-markdown renders an unterminated fence as a `<pre>` while the answer is still arriving, and nothing here gates on `loading`. A button that appears only when the turn ends is the one the user does not have while they are reading. ⛔ **THE STRUCTURAL PART IS THE POINT AND IT IS NOT REFACTORING FOR ITS OWN SAKE: BOTH MARKDOWN CALL SITES ARE NOW ONE `<Md>` RENDERER.** The completed turn (:160) and the STREAMING PREVIEW (:182) each spelled out `<div className={s.md}><ReactMarkdown …>` in full, which is exactly how a change reaches one and misses the other — the half-surface defect this repo has now shipped twice (LORAMER_LORA_WORKING_SHARED_V1 was the same shape one component up). There is exactly ONE `<ReactMarkdown>` element in the file and the guard pins it there. ⛔ **A SHIPPED CSS RULE WAS CHANGED, STATED HERE RATHER THAN BURIED IN A DIFF: `.md pre` GAINED `position: relative` AND `padding-right: 38px`.** `position` was previously unset, so an absolutely-positioned descendant would have escaped to the nearest positioned ancestor — on the shelf that is the PORTALED PANEL, i.e. the button would have floated off the block entirely rather than merely looking wrong. ⛔ **THE COPIED FLAG IS RESET-KEYED ON clientId, AND THAT IS d55f739 ONE LAYER DOWN.** The shelf stays MOUNTED inside Shell across a client switch while the page REMOUNTS, and `messages.map` keys by INDEX — so on the shelf React REUSES the component instance and its state when another client's conversation replaces it. A `copied` flag left true reads as "you copied this" over a different client's block. A `CopyResetContext` carrying clientId clears it; a local timer alone would not have. ⛔ **THE BUTTON'S OWN TEXT CAN NEVER BE COPIED, AND IT IS EXCLUDED BY NODE IDENTITY RATHER THAN BY TRIMMING THE STRING AFTERWARDS.** The button lives INSIDE the `<pre>` (which is what makes the `position: relative` change load-bearing), so the copy reads the `<code>` child's textContent, falling back to joining the childNodes with the button's node skipped via `data-lora-copy`. Reading the `<pre>`'s textContent wholesale would paste the word "Copy" into the user's negative-keyword box — the precise output this feature exists to make paste-able. ⛔ **AND THE FAILURE PATH SAYS NOTHING RATHER THAN LYING:** `clipboard.writeText` rejects without a user gesture, on an insecure origin, or when permission is denied; the catch leaves the flag false. "Copied!" over a clipboard that did not change is worse than a button that does not work, because the user pastes stale content and trusts it. **CONSTRAINTS CHECKED IN CODE, NOT ASSUMED:** icons are inline SVG via the existing `Icon` pattern and NO Tabler `ti ti-*` class exists in the file (the banked 0×0-invisible-button defect); every colour in `.copyBtn` is a `var()` WITH a literal fallback (the banked white-glyph-on-a-white-bar defect); NOTHING is portaled here (`createPortal` appears only in ChatLauncher's own chrome); NO scroll call is made, so `html { scroll-behavior: smooth }` at globals.css:126 is untouched; and legacy `/dashboard` was never opened for write. ⚠ **ONE HONEST LIMIT, WRITTEN INTO THE STYLESHEET RATHER THAN DESIGNED AROUND:** the `<pre>` scrolls horizontally and an absolutely-positioned child scrolls with it, so on a block wide enough to scroll sideways the button travels off the right edge. The alternatives each trade a real layout risk inside a shipped rule for a cosmetic gain on a case the target output does not produce — the paste-able answers this exists for are one short value per line. **FIX-WITH-GUARD, SAME COMMIT: `tests/guards/chat-copy-blocks.guard.mjs`, SEVEN LEGS, SEEN RED AGAINST PRE-FIX HEAD — 14 findings, exit 1** (run against a THROWAWAY COPY via `LORAMER_GUARD_ROOT`, real files' md5s verified identical before and after — [[★GUARD-BREAK-AND-RESTORE-IS-UNSAFE]]). It guards: the override exists and is wired; BOTH call sites go through the one `<Md>` and exactly ONE `<ReactMarkdown>` remains; no icon webfont class; the reset key exists, is provided from clientId, and clears the flag; the button node is excluded from the copy; `.md pre` carries `position: relative` and `.copyBtn`'s colours all carry fallbacks; and LEGACY reaches neither the component nor its stylesheet, so the -next-only blast radius is structural rather than merely true today. ⛔ **AND IT IS WIRED INTO `scripts/run-guards.mjs`, WHICH IS AN EXPLICIT LIST AND NOT A DIRECTORY SCAN — a guard file that is not in that list does not run, and mine did not until I added it.** Caught by the count not moving (68 total before and after), not by review. ⚙ **ONE EXISTING GUARD WAS WIDENED AND IT IS NOT A LOOSENING:** `chat-status-truthful` leg (S1) asserted `ReactMarkdown[\s\S]{0,80}streamingText`, pinning the check to the literal spelling at that call site, and it went RED when the two sites collapsed onto `<Md>`. The PROPERTY — the accumulated delta must be RENDERED AS MARKDOWN — is unchanged and now accepts either spelling, while `chat-copy-blocks` separately pins `<Md>` to being the only ReactMarkdown in the file, so the pair is strictly as strong as before. **PROVEN STILL TO BITE:** a throwaway copy rendering `<div>{streamingText}</div>` FAILS (S1). GATE-A: `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 · working tree **68 PASSED · 1 FAILED**, that one failure STILL only the held Google Tier-1 widen's own preconditions in `metrics-upsert-chunked` — **the +1 against the banked 67 is this flight's own new guard, not a regression** · `npx next build` exit 0 **run WITH the held widen present, which is a stronger proof than the usual isolation** · `check:data` exit 1 on the SAME TWO pre-existing findings (Bath Fitter google_age/google_gender not baselined; 8 retained `meta_video` stale entries), reachability unadmitted 0. ⚠ **HELD WIDEN UNTOUCHED** — blobs `e3d8a09e` / `35776e49`, re-verified after a stash cycle that a 10-minute command timeout interrupted mid-flight. ⛔ **THE WALK STAYS HALTED AND `universe_disk_headroom()` STAYS ARMED — this flight touched neither, nor any Google lane.** ⚠ **NOT DONE HERE AND THE ENTRY WARNED ABOUT IT: the button is useless until Lora EMITS fenced blocks for paste-able answers. That is FLIGHT B and it closes ★CHAT-COPY-BLOCKS; this entry does not.** | LORAMER_CHAT_COPY_BLOCKS_V1, 2026-08-06 | do not relitigate.
